# Daily Summary: 2025-03-28

### FinAudio: A Benchmark for Audio Large Language Models in Financial Applications
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.20990v1)
- **Authors**: Yupeng Cao, Haohang Li, Yangyang Yu, Shashidhar Reddy Javaji, Yueru He, Jimin Huang, Zining Zhu, Qianqian Xie, Xiao-yang Liu, Koduvayur Subbalakshmi, Meikang Qiu, Sophia Ananiadou, Jian-Yun Nie
- **Abstract**: Audio Large Language Models (AudioLLMs) have received widespread attention and have significantly improved performance on audio tasks such as conversation, audio understanding, and automatic speech recognition (ASR). Despite these advancements, there is an absence of a benchmark for assessing AudioLLMs in financial scenarios, where audio data, such as earnings conference calls and CEO speeches, are crucial resources for financial analysis and investment decisions. In this paper, we introduce \textsc{FinAudio}, the first benchmark designed to evaluate the capacity of AudioLLMs in the financial domain. We first define three tasks based on the unique characteristics of the financial domain: 1) ASR for short financial audio, 2) ASR for long financial audio, and 3) summarization of long financial audio. Then, we curate two short and two long audio datasets, respectively, and develop a novel dataset for financial audio summarization, comprising the \textsc{FinAudio} benchmark. Then, we evaluate seven prevalent AudioLLMs on \textsc{FinAudio}. Our evaluation reveals the limitations of existing AudioLLMs in the financial domain and offers insights for improving AudioLLMs. All datasets and codes will be released.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FINAUDIO, a new benchmark specifically designed for evaluating Audio Large Language Models (AudioLLMs) in the financial domain.  It addresses the current lack of suitable benchmarks for assessing the performance of AudioLLMs on financial audio data, such as earnings calls and CEO speeches, which are vital resources for financial analysis. The benchmark comprises three tasks: ASR for short financial audio clips, ASR for long financial audio recordings, and summarization of long financial audio data. The authors curated existing open-source datasets and created a novel dataset for financial audio summarization. They then evaluated seven representative AudioLLMs on FINAUDIO, revealing the limitations of current models in the financial domain.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses a Clear Gap:** The paper effectively identifies and addresses a significant gap in the existing literature – the lack of a dedicated benchmark for evaluating AudioLLMs in the financial domain. Existing benchmarks are either general-purpose or focused on textual financial data, neglecting the nuances of financial audio.
    *   **Well-Defined Tasks:** The three tasks are clearly defined and relevant to real-world financial applications. They capture key aspects of financial audio processing, from recognizing specific financial terms in short clips to summarizing lengthy discussions.
    *   **Comprehensive Dataset:** The curated and newly created datasets provide a substantial amount of financial audio data (400+ hours) for evaluation. The effort in compiling these datasets is significant and valuable.
    *   **Systematic Evaluation:** The evaluation of seven AudioLLMs is systematic and provides valuable insights into their strengths and weaknesses in the financial domain.
    *   **Open Source Availability:** Making the datasets and code publicly available fosters reproducibility and encourages further research in this area. The analysis on the open source model, Whisper-v3, and its performance compared to other closed-source models adds to the paper's value.
    *  **Prompt Robustness Analysis:** The prompt robustness is an important and often overlooked area, and it's good that the authors addressed it.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Novelty in Tasks:** While the application to finance is novel, the three tasks themselves (ASR and summarization) are standard NLP tasks. The novelty lies more in the dataset and the application domain.
    *   **Moderate Summarization Performance:** The summarization performance reported is relatively low. While the authors acknowledge this is due to the preceding ASR errors, it limits the practical value of this specific task within the benchmark as it currently stands. Future iterations should explore methods of prompting/fine-tuning the LLMs used to summarize the audio.
    *   **Limited Error Analysis:** While the error analysis identifies categories of errors, it would benefit from a more in-depth quantitative analysis of the specific types of errors and their frequency across the different models. A more thorough error analysis could offer even more actionable insights for model improvement.
    *   **Lack of In-depth Financial Interpretation:** The paper focuses primarily on the technical aspects of audio processing and benchmark creation. It could be strengthened by a deeper discussion of the financial implications of the models' performance, such as the impact of transcription errors on investment decisions or sentiment analysis.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Community Resource:** The FINAUDIO benchmark will be a valuable resource for the research community, enabling standardized evaluation and comparison of AudioLLMs for financial applications.
    *   **Practical Applications:** The benchmark can help to identify and improve AudioLLMs for various downstream tasks in the financial industry, such as automated customer service, earnings call analysis, and investment advisory.
    *   **Future Research Directions:** The findings and challenges highlighted in the paper will stimulate further research in this area, driving the development of more robust and accurate AudioLLMs for the financial domain.

*   **Novelty:**
    *   **First AudioLLM Benchmark for Finance:** The creation of FINAUDIO is the paper's main novelty. The novelty is a first step, and there is room for improvement.

**Justification:**

FINAUDIO fills a clear and significant gap in the financial AI landscape. The curated dataset, well-defined tasks, and comprehensive evaluation contribute significantly to the development of audio processing in finance. While the individual tasks are not entirely novel, their combination, application to finance, and the creation of the dataset represent a valuable contribution. The shortcomings discussed, particularly the moderate summarization performance, and limited error analysis, hold the paper back slightly.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CE
- **Score**: 7/10

### Multi-head Reward Aggregation Guided by Entropy
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.20995v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaomin Li, Xupeng Chen, Jingxuan Fan, Eric Hanchen Jiang, Mingye Gao
- **Abstract**: Aligning large language models (LLMs) with safety guidelines typically involves reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF), relying on human-generated preference annotations. However, assigning consistent overall quality ratings is challenging, prompting recent research to shift towards detailed evaluations based on multiple specific safety criteria. This paper uncovers a consistent observation: safety rules characterized by high rating entropy are generally less reliable in identifying responses preferred by humans. Leveraging this finding, we introduce ENCORE, a straightforward entropy-guided approach that composes multi-head rewards by downweighting rules exhibiting high rating entropy. Theoretically, we demonstrate that rules with elevated entropy naturally receive minimal weighting in the Bradley-Terry optimization framework, justifying our entropy-based penalization. Through extensive experiments on RewardBench safety tasks, our method significantly surpasses several competitive baselines, including random weighting, uniform weighting, single-head Bradley-Terry models, and LLM-based judging methods. Our proposed approach is training-free, broadly applicable to various datasets, and maintains interpretability, offering a practical and effective solution for multi-attribute reward modeling.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ENCORE (ENtropy-penalized Compositional REwarding), a novel approach to aggregating multi-head reward models for aligning large language models (LLMs) with safety guidelines. The core idea is that safety rules with high rating entropy (indicating less reliable and more uniform score distributions) are less accurate in predicting human preferences. ENCORE addresses this by downweighting rules with high entropy during reward aggregation. The method is training-free, broadly applicable, and maintains interpretability. Theoretical analysis and empirical evaluations on RewardBench demonstrate ENCORE's effectiveness, surpassing several baselines like uniform weighting, random weighting, single-head models, and even LLM-as-a-judge approaches. The authors also release a new multi-attribute rated dataset based on HH-RLHF and PKU-SafeRLHF.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its empirical observation and exploitation of the negative correlation between rating entropy and accuracy in multi-head reward models. While entropy has been used in various machine learning contexts, its specific application to weighting safety rules in LLM alignment is a novel contribution. The theoretical justification for entropy-based penalization adds further depth. However, the core idea of downweighting less reliable components is not entirely new in machine learning in general, so the true novelty lies in the specific application within this domain.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its practical and effective solution to a challenging problem: how to aggregate rule-specific rewards in multi-head reward models. The fact that ENCORE is training-free makes it particularly appealing, as it avoids the computational overhead and potential generalization issues associated with methods that require additional training. Outperforming LLM-as-a-judge models, despite its relatively small size of 8B parameters, underscores its potential impact. The interpretability of the entropy-based weighting scheme also makes it easier to understand the relative importance of different safety rules. This interpretability is a significant advantage over black-box weighting approaches.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong empirical results:** The paper provides compelling evidence that ENCORE outperforms several competitive baselines on RewardBench safety tasks. The consistent improvements across various datasets and settings strengthen the claims.
    *   **Theoretical justification:** The theoretical analysis based on the Bradley-Terry loss provides a solid foundation for the entropy-based penalization approach.
    *   **Training-free and interpretable:** These features make ENCORE a practical and user-friendly solution.
    *   **Generalizability:** The consistent observation of the entropy-accuracy correlation across diverse datasets suggests that ENCORE can generalize without additional tuning.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on strong LLMs for rating:** The method still relies on having access to a strong LLM judge for generating the rule-specific ratings, which could be a limitation for researchers with limited computational resources or access to the latest models. While the method is training-free for the *aggregation* of the ratings, generating those ratings remains expensive.
    *   **Limited exploration of alternative entropy measures:** While the paper briefly explores differential entropy, a more comprehensive investigation of different entropy measures might further improve performance. The discrete nature of the final ratings also needs to be acknowledged.
    *   **Scope of evaluation:** The evaluation focuses primarily on the RewardBench safety tasks. Expanding the evaluation to other alignment tasks or datasets could further demonstrate the generalizability of ENCORE.

*   **Potential Influence:** ENCORE has the potential to influence the field by providing a simple, effective, and interpretable approach to multi-head reward modeling. Its training-free nature makes it easily accessible to researchers and practitioners, and its superior performance on benchmark tasks is likely to encourage its adoption. The release of the multi-attribute rated dataset will also contribute to further research in this area. However, the method is limited in how rating scores are obtained from LLMs. This needs to be accounted for.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

ENCORE presents a genuinely useful and novel contribution to the field of LLM alignment. The idea of using entropy to guide the weighting of safety rules is both intuitive and well-supported by empirical evidence and theoretical analysis. Its key advantages (training-free, interpretable, and generally applicable) significantly increase its practical utility. However, while the novelty is appreciable, it is somewhat incremental within the broader context of machine learning techniques. Furthermore, its continued reliance on a strong LLM for the rating generation step and the relatively narrow scope of evaluation limit its overall impact. It's a valuable contribution that enhances existing methodologies, but not a fundamentally transformative breakthrough warranting a higher score. There is room to acknowledge the reliance on existing LLM judges.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Evaluating Large Language Models for Automated Clinical Abstraction in Pulmonary Embolism Registries: Performance Across Model Sizes, Versions, and Parameters
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21004v1)
- **Authors**: Mahmoud Alwakeel, Emory Buck, Jonathan G. Martin, Imran Aslam, Sudarshan Rajagopal, Jian Pei, Mihai V. Podgoreanu, Christopher J. Lindsell, An-Kwok Ian Wong
- **Abstract**: Pulmonary embolism (PE) is a leading cause of cardiovascular mortality, yet our understanding of optimal management remains limited due to heterogeneous and inaccessible radiology documentation. The PERT Consortium registry standardizes PE management data but depends on resource-intensive manual abstraction. Large language models (LLMs) offer a scalable alternative for automating concept extraction from computed tomography PE (CTPE) reports. This study evaluated the accuracy of LLMs in extracting PE-related concepts compared to a human-curated criterion standard. We retrospectively analyzed MIMIC-IV and Duke Health CTPE reports using multiple LLaMA models. Larger models (70B) outperformed smaller ones (8B), achieving kappa values of 0.98 (PE detection), 0.65-0.75 (PE location), 0.48-0.51 (right heart strain), and 0.65-0.70 (image artifacts). Moderate temperature tuning (0.2-0.5) improved accuracy, while excessive in-context examples reduced performance. A dual-model review framework achieved >80-90% precision. LLMs demonstrate strong potential for automating PE registry abstraction, minimizing manual workload while preserving accuracy.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) for automated clinical abstraction in pulmonary embolism (PE) registries, specifically focusing on computed tomography PE (CTPE) reports. It compares the performance of different LLaMA model sizes, versions, and parameter settings (temperature, in-context examples) against a human-curated standard.  The study uses data from both the MIMIC-IV and Duke University Health System (DUHS) datasets to assess the generalizability of the LLM performance. Key findings include that larger models (70B) outperform smaller models (8B) in extracting PE-related concepts, moderate temperature tuning improves accuracy, excessive in-context examples can reduce performance, and a dual-model review framework can achieve high precision. The study concludes that LLMs have strong potential for automating PE registry abstraction, reducing manual workload while preserving accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its systematic exploration of various LLaMA model configurations (size, version, temperature, multi-shot examples) for a specific and complex clinical task: extracting data for PE registries. While LLMs have been used in clinical NLP previously, this study drills down into a specific condition and systematically optimizes LLM parameters, which is a valuable contribution. The use of a dual-LLM review process is also relatively novel.

*   **Significance:** PE is a significant cause of cardiovascular mortality, and PE registries are important for understanding and improving patient outcomes. The current reliance on manual data abstraction for these registries is a major bottleneck. Automating this process with LLMs could have a significant impact on the efficiency and scalability of registry development. The findings regarding optimal model size, temperature settings, and the impact of in-context examples provide practical guidance for researchers and clinicians looking to implement LLMs for similar tasks. The dual LLM concept offers another avenue for quality control. The use of two different datasets adds to the generalizability of the findings.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The study thoroughly evaluates different LLM configurations and parameter settings.
    *   **Real-World Data:** The use of both MIMIC-IV and DUHS datasets enhances the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Clear Methodology:** The methodology is well-defined and reproducible.
    *   **Focus on a clinically important problem:** PE registry data collection is a practical clinical problem for which AI models can provide value.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited LLM selection:** The study focuses primarily on LLaMA models. Comparing with other LLMs, especially those designed for clinical NLP tasks (e.g., specialized biomedical LLMs or models trained on clinical data), could have strengthened the study. The comparison with Phi-4 14B as a second reviewer model provides a minor exploration.
    *   **Computational limitations:** The study acknowledges the limitation of not testing larger LLMs (e.g. LLaMA 3.1 405B) due to computational resource constraints. This limitation impacts the potential scope of performance exploration.
    *   **Retrospective Data:** The use of retrospective data is a limitation, as real-time clinical validation is necessary to assess the true impact of LLMs on clinical workflow.
    *   **Limited Clinical Context:** The registry data only focuses on CTPE scans. A broader evaluation on clinical notes would have been of higher value.
    *   **Unclear cost-benefit analysis:** While the reduction in manual labor is mentioned, a formal cost-benefit analysis, including the costs of compute resources and model maintenance, would strengthen the practical implications of the study.

*   **Impact and influence:** The paper contributes to the growing body of literature on LLMs in healthcare. Its systematic exploration of LLM configurations and the dual-LLM review framework provide valuable insights for researchers and clinicians in the field. However, the impact is somewhat limited by the focus on a relatively specific clinical task and the absence of real-time clinical validation. The paper's findings could influence the development of automated clinical data abstraction tools and the design of future clinical NLP studies.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:**
The paper provides a well-executed, systematic evaluation of LLMs for a practical clinical problem. While it's not revolutionary, it offers valuable insights and practical guidance that advances the field of clinical NLP. The weaknesses mentioned, such as the lack of real-time validation and limited LLM selection, prevent it from achieving a higher score. The study provides meaningful progress in a field that requires practical solutions that can be generalized across different settings. The use of Phi-4 14B as a validation LLM presents a reasonable solution for automated data auditing, although not a perfect analog for human consensus, and warrants some credit.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Can Large Language Models Predict Associations Among Human Attitudes?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21011v1)
- **Authors**: Ana Ma, Derek Powell
- **Abstract**: Prior work has shown that large language models (LLMs) can predict human attitudes based on other attitudes, but this work has largely focused on predictions from highly similar and interrelated attitudes. In contrast, human attitudes are often strongly associated even across disparate and dissimilar topics. Using a novel dataset of human responses toward diverse attitude statements, we found that a frontier language model (GPT-4o) was able to recreate the pairwise correlations among individual attitudes and to predict individuals' attitudes from one another. Crucially, in an advance over prior work, we tested GPT-4o's ability to predict in the absence of surface-similarity between attitudes, finding that while surface similarity improves prediction accuracy, the model was still highly-capable of generating meaningful social inferences between dissimilar attitudes. Altogether, our findings indicate that LLMs capture crucial aspects of the deeper, latent structure of human belief systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the ability of large language models (LLMs), specifically GPT-4o, to predict human attitudes and beliefs based on other attitudes, even when those attitudes are seemingly disparate and lack surface-level semantic similarity.  The authors created a novel dataset of human responses to diverse attitude statements and tested GPT-4o's ability to recreate pairwise correlations among attitudes and predict individual attitudes. They found that GPT-4o can predict attitudes beyond surface-level similarity, suggesting it captures aspects of the deeper, latent structure of human belief systems.  While semantic similarity improved prediction accuracy, the model's ability to make meaningful social inferences from dissimilar attitudes was a key finding.  The paper also explores the potential for LLMs to be used for persuasive purposes, raising ethical concerns.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its shift of focus from predicting attitudes based on *similar* or closely related topics (as previous research has done) to exploring predictions between *dissimilar* attitudes. This addresses a crucial gap in the existing literature, which has often focused on surface-level semantic similarities to explain LLM performance.  The creation of a new dataset specifically designed to test this aspect is also a valuable contribution.  The paper also breaks new ground by using a novel method to evaluate correlations across attitudes with the OpenAI API. This methodology is novel and can have useful applications to future research. The examination of these factors and the methodology are what elevate the significance of this paper.

**Significance:**  The findings are significant for several reasons:

*   **Understanding LLM Reasoning:** The study provides insights into the inner workings of LLMs, suggesting they are capable of more than just pattern matching based on semantic similarity. They seem to be learning some underlying structure of human belief systems and applying that in their reasoning processes.
*   **Ethical Implications:**  The paper highlights the potential for LLMs to be used for manipulative purposes by understanding and predicting individual beliefs. This is a timely and important consideration given the increasing prevalence of LLMs in various applications.
*   **Applications in Social Science:** The finding that LLMs can capture correlational structures in human data has significant implications for social science research, potentially enabling the creation of more accurate simulations of human behavior.
*   **Practical Applications:** The findings can be used to improve recommendation systems, target advertising, and align chat assistants to the cultural perspectives of their users.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Research Question:**  The paper clearly defines the problem it's addressing (predicting attitudes across dissimilar topics).
*   **Robust Methodology:** The study employs a well-designed methodology, including a custom dataset, a validated research experiment, careful construction of prompts, and rigorous statistical analysis.
*   **Thoughtful Discussion:** The paper offers a balanced and nuanced discussion of the findings, acknowledging limitations and addressing ethical considerations.
*   **Strong Supporting Evidence:** The claims made in the paper are all strongly supported by clear and robust data.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Depth on "Latent Structure":** While the paper shows LLMs can predict across dissimilar attitudes, it doesn't delve deeply into *what* this underlying "latent structure" actually is. More qualitative analysis to identify what conceptual connections the LLM is making would strengthen the study.
*   **Complexity of Metric Presentation:** Some of the experimental metrics are difficult to follow for someone not deeply versed in LLM evaluation, and the structure of the results could be improved with some clarification.

**Overall:**

The paper presents a significant contribution to the field by moving beyond simply demonstrating that LLMs can predict attitudes to exploring *how* they do it, even when surface-level similarities are absent.  The ethical implications and potential applications in social science are also important. Despite minor weaknesses in the depth of explanation and presentation, the study is well-executed and insightful.
**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**  The score reflects the strong methodology, original and important research question, and insightful findings. The significance of the paper is somewhat tempered by the limitations noted above (specifically the limited deep dive into understanding the latent structure). This score also takes into consideration the high volume of research conducted in the field of LLMs as of late, which limits the influence that research may have unless highly significant.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Scalability Evaluation of HPC Multi-GPU Training for ECG-based LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21033v1)
- **Authors**: Dimitar Mileski, Nikola Petrovski, Marjan Gusev
- **Abstract**: Training large language models requires extensive processing, made possible by many high-performance computing resources. This study compares multi-node and multi-GPU environments for training large language models of electrocardiograms. It provides a detailed mapping of current frameworks for distributed deep learning in multinode and multi-GPU settings, including Horovod from Uber, DeepSpeed from Microsoft, and the built-in distributed capabilities of PyTorch and TensorFlow. We compare various multi-GPU setups for different dataset configurations, utilizing multiple HPC nodes independently and focusing on scalability, speedup, efficiency, and overhead. The analysis leverages HPC infrastructure with SLURM, Apptainer (Singularity) containers, CUDA, PyTorch, and shell scripts to support training workflows and automation. We achieved a sub-linear speedup when scaling the number of GPUs, with values of 1.6x for two and 1.9x for four.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the scalability of High-Performance Computing (HPC) multi-GPU training for Electrocardiogram (ECG)-based Large Language Models (LLMs). It compares different multi-node and multi-GPU setups using frameworks like Horovod, DeepSpeed, PyTorch, and TensorFlow. The experiments were conducted on the MeluXina supercomputer, and the study analyzes speedup, efficiency, and overhead across various dataset configurations and GPU counts. The authors find sub-linear speedup when scaling the number of GPUs, with maximums of 1.6x for two GPUs and 1.9x for four GPUs. They also explored different CPU configurations and sliding window sizes. The results provide insights into the bottlenecks and opportunities for optimization in training ECG-based LLMs on HPC infrastructure.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** While the application of LLMs to ECG data is gaining traction, the paper's novelty lies primarily in its systematic evaluation of different distributed training frameworks and hardware configurations on a specific HPC environment for this particular use case. The individual components (ECG LLMs, distributed training, HPC) are not new, but the combination and empirical analysis contribute new information. However, the extent of this contribution should be carefully considered. The achieved speedup, 1.9x for four GPUs is modest.

**Significance:** The paper contributes practically by providing empirical guidelines for researchers and practitioners working on ECG LLMs and similar healthcare AI applications on HPC platforms.  The findings regarding optimal sliding window sizes, CPU core counts, and the performance of different GPU configurations can inform future resource allocation and model training strategies. However, the limited speedup and some inconsistencies between the presented arguments weakens the overall impact.

**Strengths:**

*   **Empirical Analysis:**  The paper presents a well-defined experimental setup with multiple configurations of CPUs, GPUs, and window sizes.
*   **Clear Methodology:** The metrics (speedup, efficiency, overhead) are clearly defined and applied.
*   **Practical Insights:** The paper offers actionable insights into the performance of different distributed training strategies for ECG LLMs, which could benefit other researchers in the field.
*   **Reproducibility:** The paper details the software stack, environment configurations, and workflow, enabling the reproducibility of the study by other researchers with access to similar resources.
*   **HPC-Specific Workflow:** It details how to submit SLURM scripts to the HPC (MeluXina).

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Speedup:** The sub-linear speedups obtained (1.6x and 1.9x) are not very impressive and raise questions about the efficiency of the chosen distributed training strategies. The discussion does not fully address the reasons for the poor scaling. Further profiling and optimization strategies are needed to overcome the bottlenecks.
*   **Lack of Multi-Node Testing:** The study mainly focuses on multi-GPU training within a single node. Exploring multi-node training setups and the impact of inter-node communication would have significantly enhanced the paper's scope and practical value. While the abstract states testing both, the empirical section and results show just single node testing. This weakens the overall argument on scalability.
*   **Dataset Specificity:**  The ECG datasets may not be representative of other healthcare or time-series data, limiting the generalizability of the findings.
*   **Missing Baselines:** There could be more extensive comparison with existing state-of-the-art methods.
*  **Limited Novelty**: While the paper highlights practical considerations for the task and setting, the lack of substantial performance gains diminishes its novelty.
*  **Lack of error bars in graphs**. The graphs do not show the variance in the results.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses outlined above, I assign the paper a score of **5**.

The paper has merit in providing practical insights for training ECG LLMs on HPC systems, documenting the experimental setup, and highlighting the workflow used at MeluXina. The detailed description of the software and environment setup is valuable for reproducibility. However, the modest speedups, lack of proper scaling experiments, dataset specificity, and failure to sufficiently delve into bottlenecks limit the overall significance and novelty of the work. A higher score would require a more substantial performance improvement and a more in-depth analysis of the scaling limitations. The lack of error bars further damages the credibility of the quantitative results. It's a worthwhile engineering effort with limited research implications.

Score: 5

- **Classification**: cs.DC
- **Score**: 5/10

### What Changed and What Could Have Changed? State-Change Counterfactuals for Procedure-Aware Video Representation Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21055v1)
- **Authors**: Chi-Hsi Kung, Frangil Ramirez, Juhyung Ha, Yi-Ting Chen, David Crandall, Yi-Hsuan Tsai
- **Abstract**: Understanding a procedural activity requires modeling both how action steps transform the scene, and how evolving scene transformations can influence the sequence of action steps, even those that are accidental or erroneous. Existing work has studied procedure-aware video representations by proposing novel approaches such as modeling the temporal order of actions and has not explicitly learned the state changes (scene transformations). In this work, we study procedure-aware video representation learning by incorporating state-change descriptions generated by Large Language Models (LLMs) as supervision signals for video encoders. Moreover, we generate state-change counterfactuals that simulate hypothesized failure outcomes, allowing models to learn by imagining the unseen ``What if'' scenarios. This counterfactual reasoning facilitates the model's ability to understand the cause and effect of each step in an activity. To verify the procedure awareness of our model, we conduct extensive experiments on procedure-aware tasks, including temporal action segmentation and error detection. Our results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed state-change descriptions and their counterfactuals and achieve significant improvements on multiple tasks. We will make our source code and data publicly available soon.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel approach for procedure-aware video representation learning by incorporating state-change descriptions and their counterfactuals. It leverages a Large Language Model (LLM) to generate descriptions of "what changed" (actual action-induced transformations) and "what could have changed" (hypothetical deviations). These descriptions, at both clip and video levels, are then used as supervision signals for video encoders via temporal contrastive learning, enabling the model to learn by "imagining the unseen." The authors evaluate their learned representations on temporal action segmentation and error detection tasks, demonstrating improved performance compared to baseline methods. The approach integrates before-and-after states and state-change counterfactuals into a hierarchical learning framework.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The main novelty lies in the explicit incorporation of state-change descriptions and *counterfactual* reasoning into video representation learning for procedures. While other work has used language models or contrastive learning, this specific combination aimed at capturing causal relationships between actions and state changes is a distinctive contribution. The hierarchical approach, addressing both clip and video levels, adds further novelty. However, similar counterfactual ideas have been explored in other domains (e.g., image editing, VQA).

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the improved performance on procedural understanding tasks like temporal action segmentation and error detection. The ability to detect errors and understand procedures is crucial for numerous real-world applications (robotics, assisted living, etc.).  While gains are demonstrated, their magnitude, given the reliance on a powerful LLM for data augmentation, requires careful consideration.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement and motivation.
    *   Well-defined methodology for generating state-change descriptions and counterfactuals using LLMs.
    *   Hierarchical learning framework addressing clip- and video-level understanding.
    *   Experimental results demonstrating improved performance on key tasks.
    *   Ablation studies providing insights into the contribution of different components.
    * Public code and dataset should enable further research in this direction.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Reliance on Large Language Models (LLMs): The core of the approach relies heavily on the quality of descriptions generated by the LLM. This raises concerns about bias, generalizability, and the potential for inaccurate or nonsensical counterfactuals to negatively influence learning. The paper lacks a thorough analysis of the LLM's generated text quality and its impact on downstream tasks.
    *   Limited exploration of LLM prompting strategies: The paper could benefit from a deeper dive into the prompt engineering used to elicit desired outputs from the LLM.  What are the limitations? How sensitive is the model to prompt variations?
    *   Incremental performance gains: While improvements are shown, they might be considered incremental, especially considering the complexities introduced by the LLM. A more thorough investigation comparing to simpler baselines that leverage existing procedure datasets, or more careful control of LLM variables would be beneficial.
    *   Limited generalizability assessment: While Ego4D is a diverse dataset, further evaluation on additional, more specialized procedural datasets (e.g., robot manipulation, industrial assembly) would strengthen the generalizability claims.
    *   Lack of explainability: The paper doesn't provide much insight into *why* the model performs better. Understanding the causal relationships learned by the model is not explored at a granular level.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper opens a promising avenue for incorporating causal reasoning into video representation learning for procedures. It could inspire future work on:
    *   Developing more robust and reliable methods for generating state-change descriptions and counterfactuals, possibly exploring alternative LLMs, few-shot prompt strategies, or retrieval augmented generation.
    *   Exploring different contrastive learning objectives tailored to state-change and counterfactual data.
    *   Applying the proposed approach to other procedural tasks, like robot manipulation and human-robot interaction.
    * Investigate methods to generate multimodal counterfactuals.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper presents a novel integration of state-change descriptions and counterfactual reasoning using LLMs, its reliance on a powerful, black-box component, and the somewhat incremental performance gains, temper the overall significance. The lack of in-depth analysis of the LLM's outputs and limited generalizability assessment further contribute to a lower score. However, the method represents a valuable step towards better procedural understanding and can provide the inspiration and techniques for future research.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Online Reasoning Video Segmentation with Just-in-Time Digital Twins
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21056v1)
- **Authors**: Yiqing Shen, Bohan Liu, Chenjia Li, Lalithkumar Seenivasan, Mathias Unberath
- **Abstract**: Reasoning segmentation (RS) aims to identify and segment objects of interest based on implicit text queries. As such, RS is a catalyst for embodied AI agents, enabling them to interpret high-level commands without requiring explicit step-by-step guidance. However, current RS approaches rely heavily on the visual perception capabilities of multimodal large language models (LLMs), leading to several major limitations. First, they struggle with queries that require multiple steps of reasoning or those that involve complex spatial/temporal relationships. Second, they necessitate LLM fine-tuning, which may require frequent updates to maintain compatibility with contemporary LLMs and may increase risks of catastrophic forgetting during fine-tuning. Finally, being primarily designed for static images or offline video processing, they scale poorly to online video data. To address these limitations, we propose an agent framework that disentangles perception and reasoning for online video RS without LLM fine-tuning. Our innovation is the introduction of a just-in-time digital twin concept, where -- given an implicit query -- a LLM plans the construction of a low-level scene representation from high-level video using specialist vision models. We refer to this approach to creating a digital twin as "just-in-time" because the LLM planner will anticipate the need for specific information and only request this limited subset instead of always evaluating every specialist model. The LLM then performs reasoning on this digital twin representation to identify target objects. To evaluate our approach, we introduce a new comprehensive video reasoning segmentation benchmark comprising 200 videos with 895 implicit text queries. The benchmark spans three reasoning categories (semantic, spatial, and temporal) with three different reasoning chain complexity.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel framework for online reasoning video segmentation (RS) called "Online Reasoning Video Segmentation with Just-in-Time Digital Twins." This framework aims to address the limitations of existing RS approaches that heavily rely on multimodal Large Language Models (LLMs). It disentangles perception and reasoning by employing an LLM planner to construct low-level scene representations (digital twins) from high-level video using specialist vision models. This approach, termed "just-in-time," selectively requests information from these models only when required by the implicit query, improving efficiency and enabling complex reasoning without LLM fine-tuning. The paper introduces a new video reasoning segmentation benchmark comprising 200 videos with 895 implicit text queries across semantic, spatial, and temporal reasoning categories. Experimental results demonstrate that the proposed method outperforms existing approaches across all reasoning categories.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the "just-in-time" digital twin concept for video RS. The idea of dynamically constructing a scene representation tailored to the specific query, as opposed to using a fixed, comprehensive representation, is a significant contribution. Disentangling perception from reasoning also allows for the integration of specialized vision models and LLMs optimized for specific roles. While digital twins are an existing concept, their application in this dynamic, query-driven, and online video segmentation setting appears to be novel. The benchmark dataset is also a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from its potential to advance embodied AI agents. By enabling efficient online RS without LLM fine-tuning, the framework can enable AI agents to understand complex, implicit instructions in real-time, dynamic environments. The improved performance on complex reasoning tasks and spatial/temporal awareness addresses a major bottleneck in existing RS systems. This can facilitate robots and other agents to interact meaningfully with the real world based on high-level instructions. By improving the online capability of RS, this work could also improve online video understanding tasks such as identifying potential hazards in self-driving car footage and automatically captioning important sections of a live stream.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Addresses limitations of current RS approaches (complex reasoning, LLM fine-tuning, online processing).
    *   Introduces a novel and efficient "just-in-time" digital twin concept.
    *   Presents a comprehensive video reasoning segmentation benchmark.
    *   Demonstrates strong experimental results across various reasoning categories and difficulty levels.
    *   Provides a clear and well-structured framework with detailed explanations.
    *   The framework doesn't require LLM fine-tuning which is a significant advantage due to the amount of resource required for finetuning, and the risk of catastrophic forgetting

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Relies on powerful pre-trained specialist vision models and LLMs. The method's performance is intrinsically tied to the capabilities of these underlying models. Future versions might face challenges as new foundation models are released that perform specific tasks better.
    *   The implementation details of the LLM-coder for generating executable spatial/temporal reasoning code could be more elaborated upon.
    *   While the experiments are comprehensive, further analysis of the computational cost and latency of the proposed method in a real-world online setting would strengthen the evaluation.
    *   The framework makes heavy use of LLMs. Though finetuning is not required, it is still very computationally expensive.

*   **Potential Influence:**
    *   The "just-in-time" digital twin concept could inspire new architectures for video understanding and reasoning.
    *   The benchmark dataset could facilitate further research and development in video RS.
    *   The framework could be extended to other embodied AI tasks.
    *   Future research could investigate ways to make the specialist models more adaptable and less reliant on powerful pre-trained models, or experiment with smaller LLMs

*   **Overall:**
    The paper presents a significant and novel contribution to the field of video reasoning segmentation. The "just-in-time" digital twin concept, coupled with the disentangled perception-reasoning framework and the comprehensive benchmark dataset, provides a valuable foundation for future research. While there are some limitations, the strengths of the paper outweigh the weaknesses.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Efficient Multi-Instance Generation with Janus-Pro-Dirven Prompt Parsing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21069v1)
- **Authors**: Fan Qi, Yu Duan, Changsheng Xu
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in text-guided diffusion models have revolutionized conditional image generation, yet they struggle to synthesize complex scenes with multiple objects due to imprecise spatial grounding and limited scalability. We address these challenges through two key modules: 1) Janus-Pro-driven Prompt Parsing, a prompt-layout parsing module that bridges text understanding and layout generation via a compact 1B-parameter architecture, and 2) MIGLoRA, a parameter-efficient plug-in integrating Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) into UNet (SD1.5) and DiT (SD3) backbones. MIGLoRA is capable of preserving the base model's parameters and ensuring plug-and-play adaptability, minimizing architectural intrusion while enabling efficient fine-tuning. To support a comprehensive evaluation, we create DescripBox and DescripBox-1024, benchmarks that span diverse scenes and resolutions. The proposed method achieves state-of-the-art performance on COCO and LVIS benchmarks while maintaining parameter efficiency, demonstrating superior layout fidelity and scalability for open-world synthesis.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel approach to multi-instance generation (MIG) in text-to-image synthesis. It tackles the limitations of existing diffusion models in creating complex scenes with multiple objects, specifically addressing imprecise spatial grounding and limited scalability. The method hinges on two key modules: (1) Janus-Pro-driven Prompt Parsing, which utilizes a lightweight LLM adapter to parse free-form prompts into structured layouts, and (2) MIGLORA, a parameter-efficient plug-in integrating Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) into diffusion backbones (SD1.5/SDXL/SD3). MIGLORA employs mask-driven feature concatenation and task-specific LoRA integration to inject spatial priors.  The authors create DescripBox and DescripBox-1024 datasets to evaluate the method across different scenes and resolutions. Experimental results on COCO and LVIS show state-of-the-art performance with parameter efficiency, demonstrating layout fidelity and scalability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents incremental but significant novelty. The use of Janus-Pro for prompt parsing is not entirely novel (as acknowledged by the authors citing previous LLM-based prompt parsing), but the specific fine-tuning and integration for layout generation using a lightweight LLM and iterative refinement framework is a contribution. The main novelty lies in the MIGLORA module and its parameter-efficient integration with multiple diffusion backbones (SD1.5/SDXL/SD3). The mask-driven feature concatenation and RoPE inspired position encoding for spatial grounding are good ideas and improve the performance. The dynamic guidance schedule with LoRA rank adjustment is also a welcome improvement.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem in text-to-image generation: creating complex scenes with multiple objects while maintaining spatial and semantic coherence. The parameter efficiency of MIGLORA is a significant advantage, making the approach more practical for resource-constrained environments and large-scale applications. The creation of the DescripBox dataset helps advance research in this area by providing a comprehensive benchmark. The state-of-the-art results on COCO and LVIS demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Parameter Efficiency:** MIGLORA achieves competitive or state-of-the-art results with significantly fewer parameters than existing methods. This is a major strength, especially considering the computational cost of diffusion models.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper presents thorough quantitative and qualitative evaluations on multiple datasets, including COCO, LVIS, and the newly introduced DescripBox datasets.
    *   **Clear and Well-Written:** The paper is well-structured and easy to follow, with clear explanations of the proposed method and experimental setup.
    *   **Adaptability:** MIGLORA is designed as a plugin, increasing adaptability to different backbones.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Innovation:** While the combination of Janus-Pro and MIGLORA is effective, some of the individual components build upon existing techniques.  The improvement brought by ROPE inspired encoding is relatively marginal.
    *   **Dataset Limitations:** While DescripBox is a valuable contribution, further details about its construction, filtering, and potential biases would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Limited Qualitative Analysis:** While qualitative results are shown, a more in-depth analysis of failure cases and the limitations of the method would be beneficial.
    *  **The Janus-Pro constraints are not well justified.** The reason for attention collapse in Janus Pro when mask counts exceed 4 is not clear. It could be more closely related to mask size than mask count.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of text-to-image generation by providing a more efficient and scalable approach to multi-instance generation. The MIGLORA module could be readily adopted by other researchers and practitioners.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

A score of 7 reflects a solid contribution with incremental but significant novelty. The work presents a good combination of a layout prior and parameter efficient training using LORA and achieves state of the art result. While the use of Janus-Pro and LoRA are not inherently novel, their adaptation and integration within MIGLORA, along with the mask-driven feature fusion and well explained ablation studies, are valuable contributions. The paper's main strength lies in its practical benefits, such as parameter efficiency and the improved spatial precision for multi-instance scenes. These factors justify a score of 7, indicating a valuable contribution to the field. However, a few limitations such as incremental innovation, limited robustness with mask and ambiguity from Janus-Pro constraints, prevent this work to be positioned among the very best in its category.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Can Video Diffusion Model Reconstruct 4D Geometry?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21082v1)
- **Authors**: Jinjie Mai, Wenxuan Zhu, Haozhe Liu, Bing Li, Cheng Zheng, Jürgen Schmidhuber, Bernard Ghanem
- **Abstract**: Reconstructing dynamic 3D scenes (i.e., 4D geometry) from monocular video is an important yet challenging problem. Conventional multiview geometry-based approaches often struggle with dynamic motion, whereas recent learning-based methods either require specialized 4D representation or sophisticated optimization. In this paper, we present Sora3R, a novel framework that taps into the rich spatiotemporal priors of large-scale video diffusion models to directly infer 4D pointmaps from casual videos. Sora3R follows a two-stage pipeline: (1) we adapt a pointmap VAE from a pretrained video VAE, ensuring compatibility between the geometry and video latent spaces; (2) we finetune a diffusion backbone in combined video and pointmap latent space to generate coherent 4D pointmaps for every frame. Sora3R operates in a fully feedforward manner, requiring no external modules (e.g., depth, optical flow, or segmentation) or iterative global alignment. Extensive experiments demonstrate that Sora3R reliably recovers both camera poses and detailed scene geometry, achieving performance on par with state-of-the-art methods for dynamic 4D reconstruction across diverse scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Sora3R, a novel framework for reconstructing dynamic 3D scenes (4D geometry) from monocular videos. The method leverages the spatiotemporal priors learned by large-scale video diffusion models. Sora3R adopts a two-stage pipeline: 1) Adapts a pointmap VAE from a pre-trained video VAE to ensure compatibility between geometry and video latent spaces. 2) Fine-tunes a diffusion backbone in the combined latent space of video and pointmap to generate coherent 4D pointmaps for each frame. The framework operates in a feedforward manner without external modules or iterative alignment. Experiments demonstrate the recovery of camera poses and scene geometry, achieving performance comparable to state-of-the-art methods for dynamic 4D reconstruction across different scenarios.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper presents a conceptually interesting approach by harnessing pre-trained video diffusion models for 4D geometry reconstruction. Several aspects contribute to its novelty, but with significant caveats:

*   **Diffusion-Based Reconstruction:** The core idea of leveraging video diffusion models, not just for generation but directly for reconstruction (especially 4D), is a noteworthy contribution. This separates the approach from traditional multiview geometry techniques and many learning-based methods that require specialized 4D representations. The paper builds upon the premise that VDMs capture implicit world knowledge that can be repurposed.
*   **Pointmap VAE Adaptation:** The fine-tuning of a pointmap VAE from a pretrained video VAE is designed to bridge the distributional gap between video frames and 4D geometry. This addresses a practical challenge in applying VDMs to geometry, and the ablation study demonstrates its effectiveness.
*   **Feedforward Inference:**  The feedforward nature of Sora3R is a potential advantage. The lack of iterative optimization could lead to faster inference compared to methods relying on global alignment or post-optimization refinement, although this benefit needs a fair comparison including the training time.

However, it is essential to consider the following limitations on novelty:

*   **Reliance on VDMs:** The entire approach hinges on the capabilities of existing VDMs (OpenSora in this case). The paper *repurposes* the VDM rather than making fundamental improvements *within* the diffusion model itself. If the underlying VDM is limited, Sora3R is also constrained.
*   **Pointmap Representation:** The choice of pointmaps as a 4D representation, while straightforward, is not revolutionary.  It builds upon existing pointmap regression approaches like DUSt3R and MonST3R. The primary contribution is in *how* the pointmaps are generated, not the representation itself.

**Significance:**

The paper's significance stems from its potential to simplify dynamic 3D reconstruction. If Sora3R reliably translates VDMs' world knowledge into accurate 4D geometry, it could have implications for several applications:

*   **Accessibility of 4D Reconstruction:** By reducing the need for massive annotated 4D datasets, Sora3R could make dynamic 3D reconstruction more accessible to researchers and practitioners with limited resources.
*   **Dynamic Scene Understanding:** The ability to reconstruct dynamic scenes robustly is crucial for robotics, AR/VR, and other areas where interacting with moving objects is essential.

However, the provided results suggest that the work is still in its early stages and that more robust performance across datasets is required. The paper's performance, while being on par with SOTA, does not outperform the SOTA. The reliance on strong pretrained video diffusion models could also be a limitation, as access to, and development of, these models is heavily concentrated within certain entities. Finally, the method has limitations regarding the camera pose estimation which affects its robustness.

**Strengths:**

*   Conceptually elegant approach leveraging VDMs.
*   The VAE adaptation addresses a key practical challenge.
*   Feedforward inference could lead to faster processing.

**Weaknesses:**

*   Performance doesn't consistently outperform existing methods and actually fails to be competitive in cases where others perform exceptionally well.
*   Reliance on a potentially computationally-heavy video diffusion model.
*   Limitations are exposed by some of the ablation studies and the qualitative analysis.
*   The paper lacks a thorough study into its performance, not directly comparing to relevant work but also through the limited datasets studied.

**Potential Influence:**

The work could encourage further research into:

*   Repurposing VDMs for geometry reconstruction and scene understanding tasks.
*   Bridging the gap between generative models and explicit geometric representations.
*   Developing more efficient and accessible 4D reconstruction techniques.

**Score:**

Score: 6/10

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel idea with a promising architecture, and the ablation studies confirm the benefits of the proposed adaptation strategy. However, the performance does not yet demonstrate a significant leap forward compared to existing methods. The paper needs to establish a convincing case for the method's robustness and generalizability through a more diverse set of experiments. This work shows a potential for simplification of a problem and shows promise but needs a few iterations of improvement to fulfill its potential.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### ZJUKLAB at SemEval-2025 Task 4: Unlearning via Model Merging
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21088v1)
- **Authors**: Haoming Xu, Shuxun Wang, Yanqiu Zhao, Yi Zhong, Ziyan Jiang, Ningyuan Zhao, Shumin Deng, Huajun Chen, Ningyu Zhang
- **Abstract**: This paper presents the ZJUKLAB team's submission for SemEval-2025 Task 4: Unlearning Sensitive Content from Large Language Models. This task aims to selectively erase sensitive knowledge from large language models, avoiding both over-forgetting and under-forgetting issues. We propose an unlearning system that leverages Model Merging (specifically TIES-Merging), combining two specialized models into a more balanced unlearned model. Our system achieves competitive results, ranking second among 26 teams, with an online score of 0.944 for Task Aggregate and 0.487 for overall Aggregate. In this paper, we also conduct local experiments and perform a comprehensive analysis of the unlearning process, examining performance trajectories, loss dynamics, and weight perspectives, along with several supplementary experiments, to understand the effectiveness of our method. Furthermore, we analyze the shortcomings of our method and evaluation metrics, emphasizing that MIA scores and ROUGE-based metrics alone are insufficient to fully evaluate successful unlearning. Finally, we emphasize the need for more comprehensive evaluation methodologies and rethinking of unlearning objectives in future research. Code is available at https://github.com/zjunlp/unlearn/tree/main/semeval25.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the ZJUKLAB paper on unlearning via model merging:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes an unlearning system for large language models (LLMs) that leverages model merging. The system trains two models with complementary biases – one over-forgetting and the other under-forgetting sensitive data – and then merges them using TIES-Merging. This aims to create a balanced unlearned model. The authors demonstrate competitive results in the SemEval-2025 Task 4, ranking second. They provide local experiment results analyzing performance trajectories, loss dynamics, and weight perspectives. The authors also acknowledge limitations of current evaluation metrics and suggest rethinking unlearning objectives.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The application of model merging, specifically TIES-Merging, to the unlearning problem is the key novelty. While model merging itself is not entirely new, its use as a strategy to combine models with complementary forgetting biases appears to be a reasonable contribution. The paper demonstrates that it can produce better results than either over-forgetting or under-forgetting alone. The use of NPO, GDR and KLR in training the initial biased models contributes less to the novelty.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses an important problem – the difficulty of achieving effective and balanced unlearning in LLMs. The model merging approach offers a practical strategy to navigate the trade-off between removing sensitive information and preserving general knowledge. The experimental results, including the second-place ranking in the SemEval task, suggest the potential of this approach. The paper’s analysis of the limitations of current evaluation metrics and the need to rethink unlearning objectives is also significant, as it highlights the complexity of evaluating the effectiveness of unlearning. The method allows achieving state-of-the-art performance simply by merging two models with complementary biases. This makes it an interesting practical method.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   A well-defined methodology that is relatively easy to implement.
    *   Demonstrated effectiveness through experimental results, including competitive performance in a shared task.
    *   Analysis of the method's behavior, including performance trajectories, loss dynamics, and weight perspectives.
    *   Critical evaluation of current evaluation metrics and unlearning objectives.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Limited novelty in individual components (NPO, GDR, KLR, TIES-Merging). The primary novelty lies in the combination of these elements.
    *   The online experiments, while ranking highly, show less impressive MIA scores than local experiments which is potentially caused by dataset discrepancies.
    *   The over-forgetting analysis reveals limitations of the system, particularly the generation of repetitive outputs and the forgetting of generic knowledge. This suggests room for improvement in the unlearning process.
    *   Limited explanation of why the particular hyperparameters and architecture choices are chosen. While they describe how they optimize the biased models and merge them, they don't provide specific reasons for why a particular model configuration was used.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The model merging approach could inspire further research on combining models with different biases for unlearning.
    *   The analysis of evaluation metrics could lead to the development of more comprehensive and reliable methods for evaluating unlearning.
    *   The discussion of unlearning objectives could stimulate a broader discussion on the goals and limitations of unlearning in AI.

**Score:** 6.5

**Rationale:**

The paper introduces a practical and effective approach to unlearning by leveraging model merging. The experimental results and analyses support its claims, and the discussion of limitations is valuable. However, the individual components lack significant novelty, and some limitations remain in the effectiveness of the unlearning process. The results are strong and there are interesting analyses in the paper, which merit the paper a 6.5. Further improvement could focus on improving the base biased models, and better explaining some of the implementation/configuration choices. A more robust investigation of the model's unlearning capabilities against sophisticated attacks, or an exploration of adaptive merging strategies based on data characteristics, would also increase the paper’s impact.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Alleviating LLM-based Generative Retrieval Hallucination in Alipay Search
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21098v1)
- **Authors**: Yedan Shen, Kaixin Wu, Yuechen Ding, Jingyuan Wen, Hong Liu, Mingjie Zhong, Zhouhan Lin, Jia Xu, Linjian Mo
- **Abstract**: Generative retrieval (GR) has revolutionized document retrieval with the advent of large language models (LLMs), and LLM-based GR is gradually being adopted by the industry. Despite its remarkable advantages and potential, LLM-based GR suffers from hallucination and generates documents that are irrelevant to the query in some instances, severely challenging its credibility in practical applications. We thereby propose an optimized GR framework designed to alleviate retrieval hallucination, which integrates knowledge distillation reasoning in model training and incorporate decision agent to further improve retrieval precision. Specifically, we employ LLMs to assess and reason GR retrieved query-document (q-d) pairs, and then distill the reasoning data as transferred knowledge to the GR model. Moreover, we utilize a decision agent as post-processing to extend the GR retrieved documents through retrieval model and select the most relevant ones from multi perspectives as the final generative retrieval result. Extensive offline experiments on real-world datasets and online A/B tests on Fund Search and Insurance Search in Alipay demonstrate our framework's superiority and effectiveness in improving search quality and conversion gains.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of hallucination in LLM-based generative retrieval (GR) systems, where the system generates documents irrelevant to the user's query.  The authors propose an optimized GR framework to alleviate this issue, incorporating two key modules: knowledge distillation reasoning and a decision agent. The knowledge distillation module uses a larger LLM to assess and reason about query-document pairs, then distills this reasoning knowledge into the GR model. The decision agent refines the retrieved documents further by leveraging a traditional retrieval model and a LLM to evaluate relevance from multiple perspectives.  Experiments on Fund Search and Insurance Search in Alipay demonstrate the framework's effectiveness in improving search quality and conversion rates.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Problem Relevance:** The paper tackles a significant and practical issue in LLM-based information retrieval – hallucination. This is a major barrier to the adoption of these models in real-world search scenarios where accuracy and reliability are paramount.
*   **Clear and Well-Defined Approach:** The proposed framework is well-structured and clearly explained. The two-module approach (knowledge distillation and decision agent) provides a sensible breakdown of the problem and a systematic solution.
*   **Practical Validation:** The online A/B testing on real-world Alipay search scenarios is a major strength. Demonstrating improvements in key search metrics like Click PV, Click UV, Trade Count, and Trade UV adds significant credibility to the work.
*   **Ablation Study:** The ablation study clearly shows the contribution of each module (reasoning and decision agent) to the overall performance. This is crucial to justify the design choices.
*   **Reproducibility:** The paper describes its methods and implementation details in sufficient detail to allow for replication.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:** The techniques themselves (knowledge distillation and using a separate agent for re-ranking) are not entirely novel in the broader machine learning field. The novelty lies in their specific application and combination within the context of LLM-based generative retrieval.
*   **Dependency on Large LLMs:** The approach relies on access to a larger, more powerful LLM for knowledge distillation and the decision agent. This might be a limitation for organizations without access to such resources.  It would be good if the results also includes experiments by employing different sizes or type of LLMs for the knowledge distillation.
*   **Generality:** While the experiments on Alipay search scenarios are promising, it's important to acknowledge that the approach may be highly tuned to the specific characteristics of these datasets and user behaviors.  It is necessary to further evaluate the models on other search scenarios with more users.
*   **Limited Comparison:**  The paper should include evaluation of other hallucination mitigation methods to support its significance.

**Significance:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field by addressing a critical challenge in LLM-based GR and demonstrating a practical solution that yields significant improvements in a real-world application. The online A/B testing is a strong indicator of the framework's value. The main contribution resides in its real-world deployment, demonstrating hallucination improvements over an existing system with measurable economic impact.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper's strengths in practical validation and addressing a relevant problem are weighed against its limited novelty and reliance on large LLMs. It introduces a practical and effective framework to alleviate hallucination in LLM-based generative retrieval. The online A/B tests on a large platform provide strong evidence of its value. However, the technical novelty is incremental, and it relies on a particular LLM architecture.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Leveraging Large Language Models for Risk Assessment in Hyperconnected Logistic Hub Network Deployment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21115v1)
- **Authors**: Yinzhu Quan, Yujia Xu, Guanlin Chen, Frederick Benaben, Benoit Montreuil
- **Abstract**: The growing emphasis on energy efficiency and environmental sustainability in global supply chains introduces new challenges in the deployment of hyperconnected logistic hub networks. In current volatile, uncertain, complex, and ambiguous (VUCA) environments, dynamic risk assessment becomes essential to ensure successful hub deployment. However, traditional methods often struggle to effectively capture and analyze unstructured information. In this paper, we design an Large Language Model (LLM)-driven risk assessment pipeline integrated with multiple analytical tools to evaluate logistic hub deployment. This framework enables LLMs to systematically identify potential risks by analyzing unstructured data, such as geopolitical instability, financial trends, historical storm events, traffic conditions, and emerging risks from news sources. These data are processed through a suite of analytical tools, which are automatically called by LLMs to support a structured and data-driven decision-making process for logistic hub selection. In addition, we design prompts that instruct LLMs to leverage these tools for assessing the feasibility of hub selection by evaluating various risk types and levels. Through risk-based similarity analysis, LLMs cluster logistic hubs with comparable risk profiles, enabling a structured approach to risk assessment. In conclusion, the framework incorporates scalability with long-term memory and enhances decision-making through explanation and interpretation, enabling comprehensive risk assessments for logistic hub deployment in hyperconnected supply chain networks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a Large Language Model (LLM)-driven framework for assessing risks associated with deploying hyperconnected logistic hub networks.  It addresses the challenge of incorporating unstructured data (e.g., news, weather reports) into risk assessment, a limitation of traditional methods. The framework integrates multiple analytical tools managed by LLMs to systematically identify risks from diverse sources.  It uses LLMs for tasks such as extracting information, summarizing data, reasoning about risk types, and generating explanations. A case study based in Georgia demonstrates how the framework clusters logistic hubs based on risk profiles, enabling more targeted resource allocation and mitigation strategies. The framework aims to enhance scalability, interpretability, and adaptability in risk assessment for these complex networks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies primarily in the **integration** of LLMs with external analytical tools to automate and enhance risk assessment in hyperconnected logistics. While LLMs have been applied in supply chain management, the specific combination of tools and the focus on logistic hub deployment risk assessment appear to be a valuable contribution. The risk-based clustering is also a useful element. However, the individual tools themselves (e.g., Wikipedia lookup, traffic data) are not novel, and the overall architecture of the framework could be seen as an application of existing LLM prompting and orchestration techniques. The novelty is thus incremental rather than revolutionary.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in the potential to improve the resilience and efficiency of hyperconnected logistics networks.  By addressing the limitations of traditional risk assessment, the framework could lead to better decision-making regarding hub placement, resource allocation, and mitigation strategies.  The ability to incorporate unstructured data is a key advantage. The Georgia case study, while limited in scope, provides preliminary evidence of the framework's effectiveness. A strength of the work is the emphasis on interpretability, which is often lacking in more traditional AI-based approaches. However, more real-world validation and comparisons against existing methods are required to fully demonstrate its impact. The framework could be scaled to analyze risk in other global locations.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Addresses a practical problem in logistics network design.
    *   Integrates LLMs with a diverse set of external tools.
    *   Emphasizes interpretability and scalability.
    *   Demonstrates the framework with a case study.
    *   Risk-based clustering offers actionable insights.
    *   Addresses the challenges of unstructured data.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The novelty is largely in the integration aspect, not in fundamental algorithmic breakthroughs.
    *   The case study is limited in scope (geographic location and number of hubs), reducing the generalizability of the findings.
    *   The paper lacks a comparative analysis against existing risk assessment methods, making it difficult to quantify the improvements offered by the framework.
    *   Limited discussion of the computational costs and scalability challenges of the LLM-driven approach.
    *   The dependence on specific APIs (e.g., NOAA, traffic data) introduces potential vulnerabilities to the framework.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence the field by encouraging more research on LLM-driven solutions for supply chain risk management. It provides a practical example of how LLMs can be used to bridge the gap between structured and unstructured data, leading to more comprehensive risk assessments. The risk-based clustering approach could also be adopted by other researchers and practitioners.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a valuable application of LLMs to a practical problem in logistics. The integration of multiple tools is well-motivated, and the case study provides preliminary evidence of the framework's effectiveness. However, the incremental nature of the novelty, the limited scope of the case study, and the lack of comparative analysis prevent it from receiving a higher score.  The weaknesses identified significantly temper the overall impact, but the paper still offers a useful contribution to the field. Its potential to enable more resilient and efficient hyperconnected logistics networks warrants recognition.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Collaborative Evolution: Multi-Round Learning Between Large and Small Language Models for Emergent Fake News Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21127v1)
- **Authors**: Ziyi Zhou, Xiaoming Zhang, Shenghan Tan, Litian Zhang, Chaozhuo Li
- **Abstract**: The proliferation of fake news on social media platforms has exerted a substantial influence on society, leading to discernible impacts and deleterious consequences. Conventional deep learning methodologies employing small language models (SLMs) suffer from the necessity for extensive supervised training and the challenge of adapting to rapidly evolving circumstances. Large language models (LLMs), despite their robust zero-shot capabilities, have fallen short in effectively identifying fake news due to a lack of pertinent demonstrations and the dynamic nature of knowledge. In this paper, a novel framework Multi-Round Collaboration Detection (MRCD) is proposed to address these aforementioned limitations. The MRCD framework is capable of enjoying the merits from both LLMs and SLMs by integrating their generalization abilities and specialized functionalities, respectively.
