Our approach features a two-stage retrieval module that selects relevant and up-to-date demonstrations and knowledge, enhancing in-context learning for better detection of emerging news events. We further design a multi-round learning framework to ensure more reliable detection results. Our framework MRCD achieves SOTA results on two real-world datasets Pheme and Twitter16, with accuracy improvements of 7.4\% and 12.8\% compared to using only SLMs, which effectively addresses the limitations of current models and improves the detection of emergent fake news.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Collaborative Evolution: Multi-Round Learning Between Large and Small Language Models for Emergent Fake News Detection" introduces a novel framework called Multi-Round Collaboration Detection (MRCD) designed to improve the detection of emergent fake news.  MRCD leverages the strengths of both Large Language Models (LLMs) and Small Language Models (SLMs). It employs a two-stage retrieval module to select relevant demonstrations and knowledge to enhance LLM in-context learning.  A multi-round learning framework is designed to refine detection results iteratively.  The framework uses the LLM's zero-shot capability and a pre-trained SLM, dividing data into clean and noisy subsets through data selection. Finally, the LLM and SLM collaborate to generalize the SLM and transform all data into clean labels. Experiments on Pheme and Twitter16 datasets demonstrate SOTA performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The core idea of combining LLMs and SLMs in an iterative, collaborative learning framework for fake news detection is relatively novel.  While prior work has used LLMs to augment SLMs, this paper attempts a more synergistic approach. The two-stage retrieval process, aimed at providing both relevant demonstrations and dynamic knowledge to the LLM, is also a solid contribution. The multi-round sample selection process is a further step of refinement.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem: the difficulty of detecting emergent fake news due to distribution shifts and limited labeled data. The ability to adapt to new information is highly valuable in this domain. The SOTA results demonstrated on real-world datasets support the framework's effectiveness. However, there is a limitation in that, there is still some manual selection for the hyperparameters in MRCD.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly defines the challenges of emergent fake news detection and the limitations of existing approaches.
    * **Well-Defined Framework:**  The MRCD framework is well-structured and its components are clearly explained.
    * **Strong Experimental Results:**  The experimental results demonstrate significant improvements over baselines on real-world datasets. The ablation studies effectively evaluate the contribution of each component of the framework.
    * **Detailed Analysis:** The paper provides a detailed analysis of the multi-round learning process and the impact of different design choices.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Complexity:** The MRCD framework involves multiple components and stages, which might make it difficult to implement and deploy in practice.
    * **Computational Cost:** The use of LLMs can be computationally expensive, potentially limiting the scalability of the approach. Although it mentions the use of 4 NVIDIA 3090 GPUs, a more detailed evaluation of computational resources needed might be necessary.
    * **Limited Generalizability Discussion:** While the datasets used are real-world, the paper could benefit from a more thorough discussion of the potential challenges of generalizing the approach to other domains or languages. The paper has no discussion of societal impact.

* **Impact:** The MRCD framework has the potential to significantly improve the detection of emergent fake news, especially in situations where labeled data is scarce. The collaborative learning approach could inspire further research on combining LLMs and SLMs for other NLP tasks.

**Score:** 7.5

**Rationale:**

The paper demonstrates a solid contribution to the field of fake news detection. The MRCD framework is novel, well-designed, and achieves SOTA results. However, the complexity of the framework and its computational cost are potential limitations. The lack of a detailed generalizability discussion is also a minor concern.  A slightly higher score could be justified if the paper included a more thorough analysis of the framework's limitations and potential societal impact. Nevertheless, the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses, making it a valuable contribution.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### MoQa: Rethinking MoE Quantization with Multi-stage Data-model Distribution Awareness
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21135v1)
- **Authors**: Zihao Zheng, Xiuping Cui, Size Zheng, Maoliang Li, Jiayu Chen, Yun, Liang, Xiang Chen
- **Abstract**: With the advances in artificial intelligence, Mix-of-Experts (MoE) has become the main form of Large Language Models (LLMs), and its demand for model compression is increasing. Quantization is an effective method that not only compresses the models but also significantly accelerates their performance. Existing quantization methods have gradually shifted the focus from parameter scaling to the analysis of data distributions. However, their analysis is designed for dense LLMs and relies on the simple one-model-all-data mapping, which is unsuitable for MoEs. This paper proposes a new quantization framework called MoQa. MoQa decouples the data-model distribution complexity of MoEs in multiple analysis stages, quantitively revealing the dynamics during sparse data activation, data-parameter mapping, and inter-expert correlations. Based on these, MoQa identifies particular experts' and parameters' significance with optimal data-model distribution awareness and proposes a series of fine-grained mix-quantization strategies adaptive to various data activation and expert combination scenarios. Moreover, MoQa discusses the limitations of existing quantization and analyzes the impact of each stage analysis, showing novel insights for MoE quantization. Experiments show that MoQa achieves a 1.69~2.18 perplexity decrease in language modeling tasks and a 1.58%~8.91% accuracy improvement in zero-shot inference tasks. We believe MoQa will play a role in future MoE construction, optimization, and compression.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "MoQa: Rethinking MoE Quantization with Multi-stage Data-model Distribution Awareness":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MoQa, a novel quantization framework specifically designed for Mix-of-Experts (MoE) models.  MoQa addresses the limitations of existing quantization methods, which are primarily designed for dense Large Language Models (LLMs) and fail to account for the unique complexities of MoEs.  The core idea is to decouple the data-model distribution complexity in MoEs into three key analysis stages: sparse data activation, data-parameter mapping, and inter-expert correlations. This decoupling allows the framework to identify the significance of individual experts and parameters with greater awareness of the underlying data distribution. Based on these insights, MoQa proposes fine-grained mix-quantization strategies that adapt to various data activation and expert combination scenarios. The paper presents experimental results demonstrating improved perplexity on language modeling tasks and accuracy on zero-shot inference tasks compared to traditional methods like GPTQ and MoEPTQ.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its multi-stage approach to analyzing and optimizing MoE quantization.  Existing methods largely treat MoEs as monolithic structures or apply random strategies, ignoring the dynamic and complex relationships between data, experts, and parameters. MoQa's approach of decoupling these relationships represents a significant advancement. Specifically, the concept of analyzing token-level utilization, re-weighting data distributions to emphasize relevant tokens, and then mapping this refined data to expert models, before finally considering inter-expert correlations, is novel.
*   **Significance:** The work has the potential to significantly impact the field of MoE compression. The improved quantization performance achieved by MoQa, without sacrificing accuracy, can make these large models more accessible and deployable in resource-constrained environments. Further, the comprehensive analysis of data-model distribution in MoEs provides valuable insights that can guide future MoE architecture design and optimization. The discussion about the limitations of current quantization approaches for MoEs and the insightful analysis of each stage contributes to the understanding of how and where the existing quantization methods fail in MoE models, leading to a more robust and accurate analysis.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Analysis:**  The multi-stage analysis is well-motivated and clearly explained.
    *   **Fine-grained Strategies:** The resulting mix-quantization strategies are tailored to the specific challenges of MoEs.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experiments demonstrate substantial improvements compared to existing methods.
    *   **Clear Writing:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Complexity:** The framework is complex and potentially challenging to implement in practice. The number of tunable hyperparameters for the multi-stage optimization would need to be carefully tuned.
    *   **Generalization:** While the results are promising, more experiments on a wider range of MoE architectures and datasets would strengthen the claim of general applicability.
    *   **Limited Scope:** The scope of the evaluation focuses on limited popular LLMs.
*   **Potential Influence:** If widely adopted, MoQa could become a standard approach for quantizing MoEs. Its insights are likely to inspire new research directions in MoE compression and optimization. It could also influence the design of new MoE architectures that are more amenable to quantization.

**Justification:**
MoQa addresses a critical and timely challenge in the field of LLMs – efficient compression and deployment of MoE models. The paper's novel multi-stage analysis and tailored quantization strategies represent a significant advancement over existing methods. Although the complexity of the approach may pose some implementation challenges, the strong empirical results and insightful analysis justify a high score. There are limitations such as the potential for increased implementation complexity.

### ChatAnyone: Stylized Real-time Portrait Video Generation with Hierarchical Motion Diffusion Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21144v1)
- **Authors**: Jinwei Qi, Chaonan Ji, Sheng Xu, Peng Zhang, Bang Zhang, Liefeng Bo
- **Abstract**: Real-time interactive video-chat portraits have been increasingly recognized as the future trend, particularly due to the remarkable progress made in text and voice chat technologies. However, existing methods primarily focus on real-time generation of head movements, but struggle to produce synchronized body motions that match these head actions. Additionally, achieving fine-grained control over the speaking style and nuances of facial expressions remains a challenge. To address these limitations, we introduce a novel framework for stylized real-time portrait video generation, enabling expressive and flexible video chat that extends from talking head to upper-body interaction. Our approach consists of the following two stages. The first stage involves efficient hierarchical motion diffusion models, that take both explicit and implicit motion representations into account based on audio inputs, which can generate a diverse range of facial expressions with stylistic control and synchronization between head and body movements. The second stage aims to generate portrait video featuring upper-body movements, including hand gestures. We inject explicit hand control signals into the generator to produce more detailed hand movements, and further perform face refinement to enhance the overall realism and expressiveness of the portrait video. Additionally, our approach supports efficient and continuous generation of upper-body portrait video in maximum 512 * 768 resolution at up to 30fps on 4090 GPU, supporting interactive video-chat in real-time. Experimental results demonstrate the capability of our approach to produce portrait videos with rich expressiveness and natural upper-body movements.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "ChatAnyone: Stylized Real-time Portrait Video Generation with Hierarchical Motion Diffusion Model" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel two-stage framework, "ChatAnyone", for generating stylized real-time portrait videos, moving beyond talking heads to include upper-body interaction. The first stage uses a hierarchical motion diffusion model to generate face and body control signals from audio inputs, considering both explicit and implicit motion representations for fine-grained expression control and style transfer. The second stage employs a hybrid control fusion generative model to generate portrait videos, incorporating explicit landmarks, implicit offsets, and explicit hand controls for accurate facial expressions and hand movements.  A face refinement module is also added. The framework supports real-time interactive video chat with efficient, continuous generation at up to 30fps on a 4090 GPU. Experiments demonstrate the system's capability to produce portrait videos with rich expressiveness and natural upper-body movements.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**
    * **Real-time performance:** The paper's focus on real-time capability is a significant strength, crucial for interactive applications like video chat. Achieving 30fps at 512x768 resolution on a readily available GPU (4090) is a practical and valuable achievement.
    * **Expressiveness and Controllability:** The hierarchical motion diffusion model's use of both explicit and implicit motion representations to achieve fine-grained control over facial expressions, stylistic expression transfer, and synchronized body movements is a notable improvement over existing methods.  The addition of hand gesture control further enhances expressiveness.
    * **Extensibility:** The framework's modularity and support for various scenarios, ranging from head-driven animation to upper-body generation, provide valuable flexibility.
    * **Hybrid Approach:** The paper's hybrid approach to motion representation, combining explicit and implicit signals, allows the model to capture subtle nuances in facial expressions and movements while still maintaining controllability.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **GAN-based Generator:** While GANs offer efficiency, they may sacrifice some visual quality compared to more recent diffusion-based generative models, which are starting to achieve real-time performance with distillation methods. The choice of GANs, despite their limitations, might be a trade-off that is necessary given computational constraints. However, recent advances may suggest exploring alternative generators.
    * **Incremental Novelty:** While the system integrates several techniques (hierarchical diffusion for motion, GAN for video generation, and hybrid control fusion), the *individual* components aren't necessarily groundbreaking. The primary novelty lies in the *integration* and the resulting real-time performance and controllability.
    * **Dataset Dependency:**  The reliance on a collected and cleaned dataset of talkshow video clips from YouTube raises questions about the generalizability of the model to other domains and styles.
    * **Subjective Evaluation:** While quantitative metrics are provided, the evaluation would benefit from user studies to assess the perceived realism, expressiveness, and engagement of the generated videos. The focus on metrics like PSNR and SSIM are important, but somewhat less informative than user study results.

* **Significance:**

The paper contributes a practical, real-time system for generating expressive portrait videos. This could have a substantial impact on interactive applications like video conferencing, virtual avatars, and augmented reality. The framework's modular design and focus on controllability make it a valuable contribution to the field. The real-time performance achieved is especially important.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a well-engineered system that addresses a relevant problem (real-time expressive portrait video generation). The combination of techniques, the emphasis on real-time performance, and the achievement of fine-grained control over facial expressions and body movements are significant contributions. However, the individual components of the system are not revolutionary, and there are other approaches that could deliver greater performance on some fronts, albeit at the cost of speed. Also, lack of a user study is a shortcoming. Therefore, while the system is well-executed and demonstrates impressive capabilities, it doesn't represent a paradigm shift.

- **Score**: 7/10

### Embedding Domain-Specific Knowledge from LLMs into the Feature Engineering Pipeline
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21155v1)
- **Authors**: João Eduardo Batista
- **Abstract**: Feature engineering is mandatory in the machine learning pipeline to obtain robust models. While evolutionary computation is well-known for its great results both in feature selection and feature construction, its methods are computationally expensive due to the large number of evaluations required to induce the final model. Part of the reason why these algorithms require a large number of evaluations is their lack of domain-specific knowledge, resulting in a lot of random guessing during evolution. In this work, we propose using Large Language Models (LLMs) as an initial feature construction step to add knowledge to the dataset. By doing so, our results show that the evolution can converge faster, saving us computational resources. The proposed approach only provides the names of the features in the dataset and the target objective to the LLM, making it usable even when working with datasets containing private data. While consistent improvements to test performance were only observed for one-third of the datasets (CSS, PM, and IM10), possibly due to problems being easily explored by LLMs, this approach only decreased the model performance in 1/77 test cases. Additionally, this work introduces the M6GP feature engineering algorithm to symbolic regression, showing it can improve the results of the random forest regressor and produce competitive results with its predecessor, M3GP.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores a hybrid feature engineering pipeline that combines Large Language Models (LLMs) and Genetic Programming (GP). The core idea is to leverage the domain knowledge embedded in LLMs to guide the initial feature construction process before employing GP for further optimization. The pipeline involves querying an LLM (specifically GPT-4o) for feature combinations based on the dataset's feature names and objective, adding these generated features to the dataset, and then using M3GP or M6GP (GP-based feature engineering algorithms) to refine the feature set and build the final model. The authors test their approach on a variety of regression and classification datasets, comparing results with and without the LLM-based feature construction step.  They also introduce symbolic regression with M6GP. The results suggest that the LLM can accelerate the learning process for GP and, in some cases, improve model performance, particularly for well-studied problems. The authors emphasize that their approach preserves data privacy by only providing feature names and objective to the LLM, avoiding the transmission of raw data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The idea of combining LLMs with GP for feature engineering is relatively novel. While LLMs are increasingly used in various AI tasks, their integration with GP for feature construction, especially with a focus on data privacy, is a distinct contribution. The work also represents a novel symbolic regression approach.

* **Significance:** The paper has several potential areas of significance:

    *   **Improved Feature Engineering:** The results suggest that LLMs can help to guide the GP search, potentially leading to faster convergence and better model performance. This could be valuable for complex datasets where feature engineering is challenging.
    *   **Data Privacy:** The privacy-preserving aspect is a notable strength.  By only providing feature names and the target objective to the LLM, the approach avoids the risk of data leakage, making it suitable for sensitive datasets.
    *   **Reduced Computational Cost:** By starting GP algorithms with features extracted by the LLM, the computational resources for training can be reduced.
    *   **Introduces Symbolic Regression with M6GP:**  Showing the use of the M6GP algorithm for symbolic regression contributes to extending the knowledge of GP applications.

* **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear problem statement and motivation:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of feature engineering and the potential benefits of combining LLMs and GP.
    *   **Well-defined pipeline:** The proposed pipeline is clearly described and easy to understand.
    *   **Comprehensive experiments:** The experiments cover a diverse set of datasets and algorithms, providing a good overview of the approach's performance.
    *   **Emphasis on data privacy:** The focus on privacy is a significant strength, as it makes the approach more practical for real-world applications.
    *   **Interesting Results:**  The improvements observed on the CSS and IM10 datasets show the promise of this method. The comparison with other configurations is comprehensive.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Inconsistent improvements:** The LLM-based feature construction doesn't consistently improve performance across all datasets.  The authors acknowledge this and suggest that it might be related to the problem being too easy or not well-suited for LLM-based feature extraction.
    *   **LLM stochasticity and limited reproducibility:** The use of an LLM as a component adds stochasticity and makes the pipeline not easily reproducible. While the authors acknowledge it, it's a serious concern.
    *   **Limited analysis of LLM contribution:**  The paper doesn't delve deeply into *why* the LLM helps in certain cases and not others.  A more detailed analysis of the feature combinations suggested by the LLM and their impact on the GP search process would be beneficial.
    *   **No comparison with simpler baselines:** The paper lacks a comparison with more classical feature engineering methods (e.g., polynomial feature expansion, feature selection methods, etc.). This would put the results into perspective.
    *   **Dependency on a specific LLM:** The use of GPT-4o could become a limitation as LLMs evolve. It would be useful to discuss the generalizability of the approach to other LLMs.

* **Overall Significance and Impact:**

The paper presents a promising approach to feature engineering that combines the strengths of LLMs and GP. While the results are not universally positive, the privacy-preserving aspect and the potential for improved performance in certain domains make it a valuable contribution. The integration of LLMs into evolutionary algorithms is a growing area of research, and this paper provides useful insights and directions for future work.

**Score:** 6.5

**Justification:**

The paper is novel in its integration of LLMs and GP for feature engineering with a focus on privacy. The experimental results are promising, although not consistently positive. The major shortcomings are the dependency on a specific LLM model and lack of reproducibility, the limited analysis of the LLM's contribution, and the lack of baselines. The integration of LLM introduces variability, it is also a moving target as the models advance.
Overall, it's a good contribution with space for future advancements to improve the results and reproducibility.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### Model as a Game: On Numerical and Spatial Consistency for Generative Games
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21172v1)
- **Authors**: Jingye Chen, Yuzhong Zhao, Yupan Huang, Lei Cui, Li Dong, Tengchao Lv, Qifeng Chen, Furu Wei
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in generative models have significantly impacted game generation. However, despite producing high-quality graphics and adequately receiving player input, existing models often fail to maintain fundamental game properties such as numerical and spatial consistency. Numerical consistency ensures gameplay mechanics correctly reflect score changes and other quantitative elements, while spatial consistency prevents jarring scene transitions, providing seamless player experiences. In this paper, we revisit the paradigm of generative games to explore what truly constitutes a Model as a Game (MaaG) with a well-developed mechanism. We begin with an empirical study on ``Traveler'', a 2D game created by an LLM featuring minimalist rules yet challenging generative models in maintaining consistency. Based on the DiT architecture, we design two specialized modules: (1) a numerical module that integrates a LogicNet to determine event triggers, with calculations processed externally as conditions for image generation; and (2) a spatial module that maintains a map of explored areas, retrieving location-specific information during generation and linking new observations to ensure continuity. Experiments across three games demonstrate that our integrated modules significantly enhance performance on consistency metrics compared to baselines, while incurring minimal time overhead during inference.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of maintaining numerical and spatial consistency in generative games, a challenge often overlooked in recent advances in the field that focus primarily on visual quality.  The authors argue that ensuring gameplay mechanics accurately reflect score changes (numerical consistency) and that previously visited areas remain recognizable (spatial consistency) are crucial for compelling player experiences. They propose a framework built upon the Diffusion Transformer (DiT) architecture, incorporating two specialized modules: a numerical module that uses a LogicNet to determine event triggers and an external logic calculator, and a spatial module that maintains a map of explored areas to retrieve location-specific information. The approach is evaluated across three minimalist 2D games (Traveler, Pong, and Pac-Man), demonstrating improved consistency compared to baseline models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's core novelty lies in explicitly addressing and tackling numerical and spatial consistency in generative games, a facet often neglected by other works primarily focused on visual fidelity. While the DiT architecture and techniques like external memory (as seen in GameGAN) exist, the *specific* design of the LogicNet for event triggering and the explicit map representation with sliding window matching provide a contribution. The detailed empirical study on Traveler to highlight these issues adds to the novelty.  However, the individual components themselves aren't entirely novel; their integration for *this specific problem* constitutes the primary advance.

* **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from recognizing the importance of gameplay consistency, which directly impacts player engagement and believability of the generated world.  By explicitly addressing these issues, the authors move beyond simply generating visually appealing scenes towards creating more interactive and coherent game experiences. The extension of the framework to multiple game types further suggests its generalizability. This is particularly important as generative AI moves from creating static assets to building more dynamic and interactive environments.
However, the games presented are relatively simple 2D environments.  The extent to which the proposed modules can scale to more complex 3D games with richer interaction mechanics remains an open question. While a solid foundation, the immediate impact may be limited by the relative simplicity of the experiments. Furthermore, the paper relies heavily on supervision. Whether the proposed modules can be trained with less supervision, e.g. via reinforcement learning, is not addressed.

* **Strengths:**
    * Clear articulation of the problem and its importance.
    * Well-designed modules with explicit mechanisms for numerical and spatial consistency.
    * Thorough evaluation with multiple metrics and games.
    * Qualitative examples effectively illustrating the benefits of the approach.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * The simplicity of the games limits the demonstration of scalability to more complex scenarios.
    *  The components themselves are not radically new.  The core innovation is in the targeted integration for consistency.
    * It is not clear what exactly is being learned in the LogicNet module.
    * Lack of exploration of less supervised training.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research by highlighting the importance of consistency in generative games. The explicit modules offer a concrete starting point for others to build upon. Future work could explore more advanced map representations, more sophisticated reasoning mechanisms, and less supervised learning methods.

**Overall Score:**

Score: 7

**Rationale:**

The paper identifies and tackles an important problem in generative games, demonstrating a solid understanding of the challenges and presenting a reasonably novel solution.  While the individual modules are not groundbreaking, their targeted integration and thorough evaluation contribute meaningfully to the field.  However, the limitations of the experimental setup and the lack of exploration for training methods prevent a higher score. The reliance on supervised training as opposed to reinforcement learning for driving game events significantly reduces the novelty, and applicability of this work to real world gaming environments. There are also some questions regarding what exactly is being learned by the LogicNet. Nevertheless, the work represents a valuable contribution to the emerging field of generative game creation, especially as a starting point for further work exploring more complex game dynamics and less supervised learning approaches.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Integrating Large Language Models For Monte Carlo Simulation of Chemical Reaction Networks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21178v1)
- **Authors**: Sadikshya Gyawali, Ashwini Mandal, Manish Dahal, Manish Awale, Sanjay Rijal, Shital Adhikari, Vaghawan Ojha
- **Abstract**: Chemical reaction network is an important method for modeling and exploring complex biological processes, bio-chemical interactions and the behavior of different dynamics in system biology. But, formulating such reaction kinetics takes considerable time. In this paper, we leverage the efficiency of modern large language models to automate the stochastic monte carlo simulation of chemical reaction networks and enable the simulation through the reaction description provided in the form of natural languages. We also integrate this process into widely used simulation tool Copasi to further give the edge and ease to the modelers and researchers. In this work, we show the efficacy and limitations of the modern large language models to parse and create reaction kinetics for modelling complex chemical reaction processes.
- **Summary**: This paper explores the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) to automate the stochastic Monte Carlo simulation of chemical reaction networks (CRNs). The authors propose a framework that combines LLMs with simulation algorithms like the Gillespie algorithm to parse natural language descriptions of reactions, convert them into mathematical representations (kinetics and stoichiometry matrices), and perform simulations. The system generates Copasi models and integrates with the Copasi software for both direct execution and user import. They validate their approach with examples from protein aggregation and chemical reaction networks, comparing the simulation results with those from original studies and showing that the system replicates the expected dynamics. The authors also explore using open-source LLMs, although these show limitations in parsing complex descriptions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents an interesting application of LLMs to a complex problem in systems biology and chemical kinetics. The core idea of automating the generation of simulation-ready models from natural language descriptions has merit, as it could significantly reduce the manual effort involved in setting up CRN simulations. The integration with Copasi, a widely used simulation tool, further enhances the practicality of the approach.

However, several aspects need critical consideration:

*   **Novelty:** While the combination of LLMs and CRN simulation is promising, the specific novelty of this work is somewhat limited. LLMs have been applied to various scientific domains, and using them for parsing and generating structured data is not entirely new. The primary contribution seems to be the integration with a specific simulation workflow.
*   **Significance:** The potential significance lies in making CRN simulations more accessible to researchers without deep expertise in kinetic modeling. The impact would depend on the accuracy, robustness, and scalability of the system. The paper demonstrates proof-of-concept implementations but lacks comprehensive benchmark studies.
*   **Validation:** The validation relies on comparing the simulation results with previously published studies. While this is a reasonable starting point, a more rigorous evaluation would involve systematically assessing the accuracy of the parsed kinetics, the sensitivity of simulation results to parsing errors, and the performance of the LLM-based system compared to manual model building by experts. Further, a more thorough investigation into the limitations and sources of error in both the LLM parsing and the downstream simulation would be valuable. It would be worthwhile for the authors to evaluate the method's capabilities by comparing the LLM generated and manually defined models and simulations for real-world CRN, if applicable.
*   **Limitations:** The reported limitations of open-source LLMs in handling complex descriptions raise concerns about the generalizability of the approach. The practical applicability of the framework would depend on the availability of sufficiently powerful LLMs and the ability to fine-tune them for the specific domain of chemical kinetics. Additionally, the reliance on LLMs introduces potential biases and interpretability challenges.
*   **Implementation Details:** The paper lacks detailed information about the specific LLM architectures used, the training data, and the parsing strategies employed. This makes it difficult to reproduce the results or assess the technical soundness of the approach.

Overall, the paper presents a promising idea with a feasible implementation, but the lack of a thorough evaluation, limited novelty, and unclear implementation details weaken its overall impact. The potential benefits are clear, but the practical utility and generalizability of the approach need further investigation.

### Leveraging LLMs with Iterative Loop Structure for Enhanced Social Intelligence in Video Question Answering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21190v1)
- **Authors**: Erika Mori, Yue Qiu, Hirokatsu Kataoka, Yoshimitsu Aoki
- **Abstract**: Social intelligence, the ability to interpret emotions, intentions, and behaviors, is essential for effective communication and adaptive responses. As robots and AI systems become more prevalent in caregiving, healthcare, and education, the demand for AI that can interact naturally with humans grows. However, creating AI that seamlessly integrates multiple modalities, such as vision and speech, remains a challenge. Current video-based methods for social intelligence rely on general video recognition or emotion recognition techniques, often overlook the unique elements inherent in human interactions. To address this, we propose the Looped Video Debating (LVD) framework, which integrates Large Language Models (LLMs) with visual information, such as facial expressions and body movements, to enhance the transparency and reliability of question-answering tasks involving human interaction videos. Our results on the Social-IQ 2.0 benchmark show that LVD achieves state-of-the-art performance without fine-tuning. Furthermore, supplementary human annotations on existing datasets provide insights into the model's accuracy, guiding future improvements in AI-driven social intelligence.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Looped Video Debating (LVD), a framework for enhancing social intelligence in video question answering (VQA) tasks. LVD leverages large language models (LLMs) in conjunction with a VQA model in an iterative loop. The LLM first determines if a question about a human interaction video is answerable based on given information (frames and transcript). If not, it infers what additional visual information is needed (facial expression, scene context, etc.) and retrieves the relevant video frame using the timestamps inferred by the LLM. The VQA model provides a description of the retrieved image, and this description is then fed back into the LLM to answer the original question. The authors also construct a manually annotated sub-dataset of Social-IQ 2.0 to evaluate the accuracy and rationale behind LLM's responses. Experimental results on Social-IQ 2.0 demonstrate that LVD achieves state-of-the-art performance without fine-tuning, and the annotations provide insights into model accuracy and areas for improvement.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the iterative loop structure that combines LLMs and VQA models to improve social intelligence in video understanding. The "unanswerable" option and the subsequent retrieval of relevant visual information are innovative. Using LLMs for this purpose is relatively new. However, using LLMs combined with VQA systems is itself not groundbreaking, though applying this concept to social intelligence and constructing the specific LVD framework exhibits originality.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from addressing the challenge of effectively integrating multiple modalities (vision and language) for socially intelligent AI. The LVD framework shows promising results in improving QA accuracy on the Social-IQ 2.0 dataset. The construction of the Social-IQ Sub dataset with human annotations is also a valuable contribution that allows for deeper analysis of the models' reasoning processes and identification of failure modes. Furthermore, a detailed analysis of different LLMs is presented, leading to a better understanding of the current capabilities and shortcomings of these models in the area of social intelligence.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Well-defined problem and clear motivation.
    *   Novel LVD framework for enhancing social intelligence in VQA.
    *   Use of LLMs with VQA models is well thought out.
    *   Construction of a valuable, manually annotated sub-dataset for detailed analysis.
    *   Achieves state-of-the-art results on Social-IQ 2.0 without fine-tuning, indicating strong generalization capabilities.
    *   Thorough analysis of results with human comparison.
    *   Clear presentation and well-organized structure.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The reliance on transcript data might limit the applicability to scenarios where transcriptions are unavailable or inaccurate (though the authors acknowledge and address transcription inaccuracies via improved methods).
    *   The improvement, while significant in some cases, is not a massive leap over existing methods. Further investigation into why LLMs are unable to accurately answer certain questions could further enhance the impact of this method.
    *   The types of visual information that can be retrieved in the loop are limited to a single image, potentially missing dynamic cues captured in video. The authors acknowledge this limitation.
    *   The study may not be fully representative of a real-world system. Datasets are inherently limited representations of reality.
    *   The choice of predefined options might introduce biases and limit the exploration of novel aspects that could enhance further model performance.

*   **Potential Influence:** The LVD framework can inspire further research in integrating LLMs with visual models for social intelligence tasks. The Social-IQ Sub dataset could serve as a valuable resource for benchmarking and analyzing future models. The findings highlight the importance of considering both visual and linguistic information for understanding human interactions, and may influence the design of more robust and interpretable AI systems.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel framework that effectively addresses a critical challenge in AI - enabling systems to exhibit social intelligence in understanding human interactions from video. While it relies on existing components (LLMs and VQA models), the way they are combined in an iterative loop is innovative and demonstrates clear improvements in accuracy without fine-tuning. The annotated dataset and the detailed analysis further contribute to the significance of the work. The weaknesses identified are acknowledged and can be addressed in future work. Taking all factors into account, the paper makes a meaningful contribution to the field.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### UGen: Unified Autoregressive Multimodal Model with Progressive Vocabulary Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21193v1)
- **Authors**: Hongxuan Tang, Hao Liu, Xinyan Xiao
- **Abstract**: We introduce UGen, a unified autoregressive multimodal model that demonstrates strong performance across text processing, image understanding, and image generation tasks simultaneously. UGen converts both texts and images into discrete token sequences and utilizes a single transformer to generate them uniformly in an autoregressive manner. To address the challenges associated with unified multimodal learning, UGen is trained using a novel mechanism, namely progressive vocabulary learning. In this process, visual token IDs are incrementally activated and integrated into the training phase, ultimately enhancing the effectiveness of unified multimodal learning. Experiments on comprehensive text and image tasks show that UGen achieves a significant overall performance improvement of 13.3% compared to the vanilla unified autoregressive method, and it also delivers competitive results across all tasks against several task-specific models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces UGen, a unified autoregressive multimodal model capable of handling text processing, image understanding, and image generation tasks.  UGen tokenizes both text and images and uses a single transformer to generate these sequences autoregressively. A key contribution is the "progressive vocabulary learning" mechanism, where visual token IDs are incrementally activated and integrated during training.  Experiments demonstrate that UGen outperforms a vanilla unified autoregressive model and achieves competitive results against task-specific models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of a unified autoregressive model for multimodal tasks isn't entirely new, as the paper itself acknowledges. The novelty lies primarily in the *progressive vocabulary learning* strategy. Incrementally activating visual tokens is a reasonable approach to mitigate interference between modalities during joint training. This idea is practical and could have merit.

*   **Significance:** The potential impact is moderate. The reported performance improvements compared to a vanilla unified model are significant (13.3% overall). However, the comparison to task-specific models reveals that UGen still lags behind in some areas. If this improvement trend can persist with increasing scale, then the simplicity of unified architecture may be valuable.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Simplicity:** The core philosophy of UGen is appealing.  It strives for a clean and direct approach without additional architectural complexities often found in multimodal models.
    *   **Progressive Vocabulary Learning:** This is a well-articulated method to address challenges of joint training of modalities.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides experiments on diverse text and image tasks.
    *   **Warm-started training**: The results of training from scratch also show an advantage of UGen compared to vanilla models, which further demonstrates the effectiveness of UGen.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Novelty of Architecture:** Autoregressive multimodal models exist (cited in the paper). Incremental vocabulary learning is the key contribution.
    *   **Model Scale and Data Limitations:** The paper acknowledges the performance gap compared to SOTA (Janus) and attributes this to data and resource limitations. The claims could be stronger if the authors could demonstrate UGen's scalability.
    *   **Lack of ablation experiments:** It could be improved by adding ablation experiments to compare different visual token IDs' activation speeds, and the ratios of different modality data during the pre-training stage.

*   **Potential Influence:** If UGen's simplicity and competitive performance can be maintained at larger scales, it could influence the field to consider simpler, more unified architectures. Further analysis of why progressive vocabulary learning works is also a contribution in itself.

*   **Rigorous Rationale for Score:** The incremental vocabulary learning and comprehensive evaluations merit a slightly above-average score. This method is efficient for the deep fusion among modalities, therefore achieves superior performance compared to baseline models. However, there are issues like data limitations, lack of ablation experiments, and some inferiority to task-specific models.

### System-wide Instrument Transformer Calibration and Line Parameter Estimation Using PMU Data
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21202v1)
- **Authors**: Antos Cheeramban Varghese, Anamitra Pal
- **Abstract**: Uncalibrated instrument transformers (ITs) can degrade the performance of downstream applications that rely on the voltage and current measurements that ITs provide. It is also well-known that phasor measurement unit (PMU)-based system-wide IT calibration and line parameter estimation (LPE) are interdependent problems. In this paper, we present a statistical framework for solving the simultaneous LPE and IT calibration (SLIC) problem using synchrophasor data. The proposed approach not only avoids the need for a perfect IT by judiciously placing a revenue quality meter (which is an expensive but non-perfect IT), but also accounts for the variations typically occurring in the line parameters. The results obtained using the IEEE 118-bus system as well as actual power system data demonstrate the high accuracy, robustness, and practical utility of the proposed approach.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of simultaneously calibrating instrument transformers (ITs) and estimating line parameters (LPE) in power systems using phasor measurement unit (PMU) data.  It proposes a statistical framework to solve the interdependent SLIC problem. The key contributions are: a quantization procedure for uniquely identifying line parameters, an algorithm to leverage the accuracy of Revenue Quality Meters (RQMs) for system-wide SLIC using only *one* RQM in a connected tree, and a method to determine the optimal RQM location for minimizing estimation errors. The approach accounts for variations in line parameters and errors inherent in PMU devices. The paper demonstrates the accuracy and robustness of the method using the IEEE 118-bus system, and validates its practicality with real-world PMU data from a U.S. power utility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The paper presents several novel aspects:

    *   **Quantization Procedure:** The proposed method's novelty lies in its quantization procedure to uniquely identify line parameters, which directly addresses the 'underdetermined' nature of the traditional problem formulations. This is a significant step towards a practical and computationally feasible solution.
    *   **Minimal RQM Requirement:** The approach reduces reliance on expensive "perfect" ITs (RQMs) and demonstrates that system-wide calibration is achievable with just *one* strategically placed RQM.  This is a cost-effective and significant contribution. Previous approaches often assume or require error-free measurements from multiple ITs or knowledge of line parameters beforehand.
    *   **RQM Placement Algorithm:**  Developing an algorithm to determine the "best" location for that single RQM is a unique contribution that further enhances the practicality of the approach.
    *   **Explicit Noise Modeling:** explicitly modeling of the PMU device additive Gaussian noise in the positive-sequence components of the voltage and current phasors. This is crucial for practical applicability, as it provides better estimations.

*   **Significance:** The work is significant for several reasons:

    *   **Improved Accuracy and Reliability:** Calibrating ITs is crucial for ensuring the accuracy of power system monitoring and control applications, leading to more reliable operation.
    *   **Practical Applicability:**  The method's ability to work with real-world data, requiring only *one* RQM, accounts for line parameter variations, and noise makes it highly relevant for practical implementation by power utilities.
    *   **Addresses a Fundamental Interdependency:** The simultaneous LPE and IT calibration problem has been a long-standing challenge. This research presents a comprehensive and effective solution that breaks the cycle of interdependency. The paper's comparison to state-of-the-art [19] clearly displays a significant leap in IT estimation.
    *   The paper makes it easily implementable by sharing the pseudo-code of the proposed algorithms.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Rigorous Methodology:**  The approach is based on solid statistical principles (TLS) and a well-defined mathematical formulation.
    *   **Comprehensive Validation:**  The use of both a standard test system (IEEE 118-bus) and real-world data provides strong evidence of the method's effectiveness.
    *   **Sensitivity Analysis:**  The sensitivity studies examining the impact of PMU noise and IT accuracy class provide valuable insights into the robustness of the method.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and logically organized, making it easy to understand the proposed method and its results.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Connected Tree Assumption:**  The requirement of a 'connected tree' topology may not always be satisfied in complex power systems. The paper could benefit from a discussion on how the method could be adapted or extended to handle more general network topologies with loops. While PMUs are commonly placed to satisfy this assumption, a brief consideration of this limitation is warranted.
    *   **Database Dependency:** reliance on prior information regarding historical value ranges of the network parameters which can introduce external dependencies on system components.
    *   **Computational Cost:** The algorithm for RQM placement (Algorithm 4), involving repeated execution of the SW-SLIC algorithm, could be computationally expensive for very large-scale systems. While centrality measures are suggested, a more detailed analysis of the computational complexity and scalability would be beneficial.
    *   **Limited types of variations are considered in the database values:** Line parameters will exhibit variations that have seasonality in them due to temperature and loading conditions. A detailed analysis of the selection of appropriate parameter variation limits must be described.

* **Potential Influence:** This research has the potential to significantly impact the field of power system monitoring and control.  Its practicality, accuracy, and robustness make it a valuable tool for power utilities seeking to improve the accuracy of their state estimation and other applications reliant on PMU data. The use of a minimal number of RQMs can drastically lower the cost of wide-area applications that use synchrophasors.

*Overall, this is a valuable contribution with sound principles that will motivate more research.*

**Score: 8.5**

*Rigorous Rationale:* The score of 8.5 reflects the significant novelty, practical relevance, and comprehensive validation of the research. The paper addresses a long-standing problem in a cost-effective and robust manner. It demonstrates a clear advancement over existing methods, particularly in its ability to function effectively with just one RQM and in accounting for line parameter variations. The main deductions in the score are due to the limitations regarding the connected tree topology and the database dependencies, as well as a lack of a more detailed scalability analysis. In the absence of these limitations, it would easily warrant an excellent rating. However, the paper is well-thought-out and provides considerable advancements in the accuracy of the measurements.

- **Classification**: eess.SY
- **Score**: 8/10

### Resource-Efficient Federated Fine-Tuning Large Language Models for Heterogeneous Data
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21213v1)
- **Authors**: Jun Liu, Yunming Liao, Hongli Xu, Yang Xu
- **Abstract**: Fine-tuning large language models (LLMs) via federated learning, i.e., FedLLM, has been proposed to adapt LLMs for various downstream applications in a privacy-preserving way. To reduce the fine-tuning costs on resource-constrained devices, FedLoRA is proposed to fine-tune only a small subset of model parameters by integrating low-rank adaptation (LoRA) into FedLLM. However, apart from resource constraints, there is still another critical challenge, i.e., data heterogeneity, severely hindering the implementation of FedLoRA in practical applications. Herein, inspired by the previous group-based federated learning paradigm, we propose a hierarchical FedLoRA framework, termed HierFedLoRA, to address these challenges. Specifically, HierFedLoRA partitions all devices into multiple near-IID groups and adjusts the intra-group aggregation frequency for each group to eliminate the negative effects of non-IID data. Meanwhile, to reduce the computation and communication cost, HierFedLoRA dynamically assigns diverse and suitable fine-tuning depth (i.e., the number of continuous fine-tuning layers from the output) for each group. HierFedLoRA explores jointly optimizing aggregation frequency and depth upon their coupled relationship to better enhance the performance of FedLoRA. Extensive experiments are conducted on a physical platform with 80 commercial devices. The results show that HierFedLoRA improves the final model accuracy by 1.6% to 4.2%, speeding up the fine-tuning process by at least 2.1$\times$, compared to the strong baselines.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a concise summary of the paper followed by a rigorous, critical evaluation of its novelty and significance, along with a justified score.

**Summary:**

The paper "Resource-Efficient Federated Fine-Tuning Large Language Models for Heterogeneous Data" addresses the challenges of fine-tuning large language models (LLMs) in a federated learning (FL) setting, specifically focusing on resource constraints and data heterogeneity. The authors propose a hierarchical federated learning framework called HierFedLoRA, which builds upon FedLoRA (federated learning with Low-Rank Adaptation) to mitigate the limitations of standard FedLoRA. HierFedLoRA partitions devices into near-IID (Independent and Identically Distributed) groups, adjusts aggregation frequency within each group, and dynamically assigns fine-tuning depth (number of layers to fine-tune) to each group based on device capabilities. The framework aims to optimize a balance between fine-tuning performance and resource consumption while addressing the negative impact of non-IID data. The authors validate their approach through experiments on a physical platform using commercial devices.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   *Practical Problem Addressed:* The paper tackles a relevant and significant challenge in the field of federated learning: adapting large language models to real-world, resource-constrained devices with heterogeneous data. This is a crucial step towards broader adoption of FL for LLMs in mobile and edge computing scenarios.
    *   *Well-Defined Approach:* The HierFedLoRA framework provides a structured and technically sound approach to addressing the identified challenges. The hierarchical grouping, adaptive aggregation frequency, and dynamic depth assignment are logical and potentially effective components.
    *   *Comprehensive Experimental Evaluation:* The experimental setup on a physical platform with commercial devices is a major strength. This provides realistic and tangible evidence of the framework's performance, rather than relying solely on simulations. The variety of datasets and tasks (sentiment analysis, question answering, semantic equivalence, and textual entailment) further strengthens the empirical validation.
    *   *Performance Gains:* The paper demonstrates substantial improvements in model accuracy and fine-tuning speed compared to strong baselines. The results showcase the effectiveness of HierFedLoRA in enhancing FedLoRA's capabilities.
    *   *Detailed Ablation Studies:* The ablation studies analyzing the individual contributions of aggregation frequency optimization and depth adaptation provide valuable insights into the framework's operation and validate the design choices.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   *Incremental Novelty:* While the paper combines existing concepts (FedLoRA, hierarchical FL, low-rank adaptation), the novelty lies primarily in the specific combination and the joint optimization strategy. The individual components are not groundbreaking, but the integration is what makes the paper worthwhile.
    *   *Complexity:* The HierFedLoRA framework introduces significant complexity compared to traditional FedLoRA. The algorithm for device grouping, frequency and depth optimization adds overhead to the system. The paper could benefit from a more detailed analysis of the computational cost of the proposed system.
    *   *Scalability concerns for Device Grouping* Although the authors divide the devices using the K-means algorithm with k = n/10. This means that the grouping cost has O(n^2). The performance may decline when the system involves millions of devices.

    *   *Limited Generalizability analysis:* While experiments using real devices are a strong point, the results are specific to the chosen datasets, model architectures (RoBERTa and DeBERTa), and device configurations. Further analysis is needed to assess how well HierFedLoRA generalizes to other LLMs and device types.
    *   *Hyperparameter Sensitivity:* The performance of HierFedLoRA likely depends on the appropriate tuning of several hyperparameters (e.g., learning rate, aggregation frequency penalty, depth penalty, number of layers in LoRA). The paper lacks a detailed analysis of hyperparameter sensitivity and practical guidelines for setting these parameters in different environments.
    *   *Limited Scope* The paper focuses primarily on optimizing resource consumption and communication efficiency but does not delve deeply into fairness considerations, which are a crucial aspect of federated learning.
*   **Significance:**

    *   *Practical Impact:* The paper has the potential to significantly impact the deployment of LLMs on edge devices, enabling more personalized and privacy-preserving applications.
    *   *Contribution to Federated Learning Research:* The research provides a valuable contribution to the ongoing effort to adapt LLMs for federated learning, offering a novel framework and practical insights.
    *   *Potential for Future Work:* The paper opens up several avenues for future research, including exploring alternative device grouping strategies, investigating the trade-offs between accuracy and fairness, and developing more sophisticated depth optimization algorithms.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper provides a valuable and practical contribution to the field of federated learning for large language models. The HierFedLoRA framework offers a well-structured approach to addressing the crucial challenges of resource constraints and data heterogeneity. The experimental validation on a physical platform provides strong evidence of the framework's effectiveness. While the core concepts are not entirely novel, the specific combination and optimization strategy represent a worthwhile advancement. However, the paper has weaknesses including the complexity of the algorithm, the limited scope of generalizability analysis, and sensitivity to hyperparameters. The novelty is somewhat incremental, but the experimental validation is thorough. Given the practicality of the paper and the solid experimental results, but also considering the weaknesses discussed, a score of 7.5 seems appropriate.

**Score: 7.5**
- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### GenFusion: Closing the Loop between Reconstruction and Generation via Videos
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21219v1)
- **Authors**: Sibo Wu, Congrong Xu, Binbin Huang, Andreas Geiger, Anpei Chen
- **Abstract**: Recently, 3D reconstruction and generation have demonstrated impressive novel view synthesis results, achieving high fidelity and efficiency. However, a notable conditioning gap can be observed between these two fields, e.g., scalable 3D scene reconstruction often requires densely captured views, whereas 3D generation typically relies on a single or no input view, which significantly limits their applications. We found that the source of this phenomenon lies in the misalignment between 3D constraints and generative priors. To address this problem, we propose a reconstruction-driven video diffusion model that learns to condition video frames on artifact-prone RGB-D renderings. Moreover, we propose a cyclical fusion pipeline that iteratively adds restoration frames from the generative model to the training set, enabling progressive expansion and addressing the viewpoint saturation limitations seen in previous reconstruction and generation pipelines. Our evaluation, including view synthesis from sparse view and masked input, validates the effectiveness of our approach.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the GenFusion paper, focusing on novelty and significance, and assigning a score with a detailed justification.

**Summary:**

GenFusion proposes a novel method for generating high-quality 3D assets and novel view synthesis, specifically addressing the conditioning gap between 3D reconstruction and 3D generation. It leverages a reconstruction-driven video diffusion model, conditioning video frames on artifact-prone RGB-D renderings. A cyclical fusion pipeline iteratively adds restoration frames from the generative model to the training set, enabling progressive expansion and addressing viewpoint saturation limitations common in previous approaches. The method shows effectiveness in view synthesis from sparse views and masked input scenarios. Key contributions include: (1) a reconstruction-driven video diffusion model for artifact repair and content generation in occluded regions; (2) a masked 3D reconstruction for artifact-GT video pair generation, forming a new evaluation protocol; and (3) demonstrated effectiveness on benchmark datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses a Real Problem:** The paper directly confronts the conditioning gap between reconstruction and generation, which is a valid and important challenge. Sparse-view reconstruction is a practical scenario, and the approach to improve it using generative priors is well-motivated.
    *   **Novel Combination of Techniques:** Combining video diffusion models with 3D reconstruction in a cyclical manner is a novel approach. It provides a way to leverage the strengths of both techniques: reconstruction provides geometric constraints, and diffusion models offer generative priors for plausibility.
    *   **Masked Reconstruction for Data Generation:** The idea of using masked 3D reconstruction to generate artifact-prone training data is clever.  It directly teaches the diffusion model to handle common reconstruction errors. The evaluation protocol is also a valuable contribution for sparse-view settings.
    *   **Strong Results:** The experiments demonstrate that GenFusion outperforms existing methods on challenging datasets, indicating that the proposed approach is effective. Quantitative comparisons are thorough, and qualitative results show artifact reduction and content completion.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Existing Models:** The method builds upon existing components like DynamiCrafter and pre-trained VAEs. This isn't necessarily a flaw, but it limits the novelty of individual components. The core novelty lies in the *integration* of these models.
    *   **Complexity and Training Time:** The cyclical training process may be computationally expensive.  The paper mentions the training time, but a more detailed analysis of the computational overhead compared to other methods would be valuable.
    *   **Limited Generalization Assessment:** Although the paper uses various datasets, a deeper dive into the model's performance on truly unseen data (different object categories, lighting conditions, or scene structures) would further strengthen the robustness claims.
    *   **Incremental Improvement over ReconFusion:** The paper aligns closely with ReconFusion [49]. The contribution of this paper should be significantly more important to warrant a publication.

*   **Novelty:** The novelty is reasonably good but not groundbreaking. The core concept of using a diffusion model to regularize reconstruction is already explored in ReconFusion, but this paper extends it to a *video* diffusion model with cyclical fusion and masked reconstruction. The video aspect and fusion pipeline are the main areas of novelty. This pushes the area forward but not in a fundamental manner.

*   **Significance:** The significance is solid. Artifact-free 3D asset generation has many applications. Providing a method that is effective with sparse views significantly lowers the barrier to entry. A better understanding of failure modes and limitations would further enhance the real-world significance. The new data generation and evaluation protocol contributes to the research community.

*   **Potential Influence:** GenFusion is likely to influence the field by demonstrating the effectiveness of combining video diffusion models and 3D reconstruction for artifact removal and content completion. The masked reconstruction approach could become a standard technique for training generative models for sparse-view reconstruction. Other researchers might build upon the proposed cyclical fusion pipeline for more complex 3D scene generation tasks. The quantitative performance increases on established benchmarks helps too.

**Score:** 6.5/10

**Rationale:**
The paper presents a solid contribution with a novel combination of techniques to address a practically relevant problem. The experimental results validate the effectiveness of the approach. The main detractor is the reliance on existing models as opposed to more fundamental novelty, and the incremental nature of the advance relative to ReconFusion. The lack of detail with limitations of the approach is also something that could be improved. Although the improvements are not large, there are enough of them across the key tasks to warrant the score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Rethinking Graph Structure Learning in the Era of LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21223v1)
- **Authors**: Zhihan Zhang, Xunkai Li, Guang Zeng, Hongchao Qin, Ronghua Li, Guoren Wang
- **Abstract**: Recently, the emergence of large language models (LLMs) has prompted researchers to explore the integration of language descriptions into graphs, aiming to enhance model encoding capabilities from a data-centric perspective. This graph representation is called text-attributed graphs (TAGs). A review of prior advancements highlights that graph structure learning (GSL) is a pivotal technique for improving data utility, making it highly relevant to efficient TAG learning. However, most GSL methods are tailored for traditional graphs without textual information, underscoring the necessity of developing a new GSL paradigm. Despite clear motivations, it remains challenging: (1) How can we define a reasonable optimization objective for GSL in the era of LLMs, considering the massive parameters in LLM? (2) How can we design an efficient model architecture that enables seamless integration of LLM for this optimization objective? For Question 1, we reformulate existing GSL optimization objectives as a tree optimization framework, shifting the focus from obtaining a well-trained edge predictor to a language-aware tree sampler. For Question 2, we propose decoupled and training-free model design principles for LLM integration, shifting the focus from computation-intensive fine-tuning to more efficient inference. Based on this, we propose Large Language and Tree Assistant (LLaTA), which leverages tree-based LLM in-context learning to enhance the understanding of topology and text, enabling reliable inference and generating improved graph structure. Extensive experiments on 10 TAG datasets demonstrate that LLaTA enjoys flexibility - incorporated with any backbone; scalability - outperforms other LLM-based GSL methods in terms of running efficiency; effectiveness - achieves SOTA performance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Rethinking Graph Structure Learning in the Era of LLMs" proposes a new paradigm for graph structure learning (GSL) specifically designed for text-attributed graphs (TAGs) leveraging large language models (LLMs). Recognizing the limitations of existing GSL methods tailored for traditional graphs without textual information, the authors introduce Large Language and Tree Assistant (LLaTA). LLaTA reformulates the GSL optimization objective as a tree-based optimization task with a language-aware tree sampler. This approach employs a structural encoding tree to capture graph topology, followed by LLM-based in-context learning to understand topology and text, and ultimately performs leaf-oriented two-step sampling for edge addition/removal.  The design aims for a decoupled, training-free model architecture, emphasizing reliable LLM inference over fine-tuning. Extensive experiments on 10 TAG datasets demonstrate LLaTA's flexibility, scalability, and state-of-the-art performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel approach to GSL for TAGs by shifting the focus from end-to-end training of edge predictors to a tree-based optimization framework driven by LLM in-context learning. The key innovations lie in the tree-based structural encoding to guide the LLM and the decoupled training-free architecture. This is a valuable contribution as prior LLM-based GSL methods often rely on fine-tuning or complex instruction datasets, hindering efficiency and adaptability.
    The key idea is to harness LLM's in-context learning abilities efficiently without computationally expensive fine-tuning, which provides a significant leap in terms of resource usage. The tree representation, while rooted in existing entropy-based methods, offers a structured framework for integrating textual data and prompting the LLM. This offers clear practical advantages.

*   **Significance:**  The paper is significant because it addresses a critical challenge in graph machine learning – efficient and robust learning with TAGs, a data representation becoming increasingly important.  By decoupling the GSL process from the need for extensive training and customized backbones, the approach makes GSL more accessible and adaptable to real-world applications. The experiments demonstrate substantial performance improvements and efficiency gains compared to existing LLM-based GSL methods.
    The effectiveness of LLaTA across a range of datasets supports its generalizability, and the ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contributions of different components. The robustness analysis and the analysis of time complexity makes this paper valuable to the community. However, the dependence on a high-performing LLM could be a limitation, as the quality of the LLM directly impacts the performance of LLaTA. Future work should explore how to make the model more robust to the LLM chosen.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing GSL methods when applied to TAGs in the era of LLMs.
    *   **Innovative Approach:**  The tree-based optimization framework and the decoupled, training-free architecture are innovative and well-motivated.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:**  The extensive experiments on 10 TAG datasets demonstrate LLaTA's superior performance, flexibility, and scalability. The ablation studies and robustness analysis provide valuable insights.
    *   **Well-written:** The paper is generally well-written and organized, making it easy to understand the proposed approach and its benefits.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **LLM Dependency:** The performance is heavily dependent on the quality and capabilities of the underlying LLM. While this is inherent to the approach, it is a potential limitation.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity**: There is sensitivity to the hyperparamter tuning which could be problematic depending on the LLM used.

*   **Potential Influence:** LLaTA has the potential to significantly influence the field of GSL, particularly in applications involving TAGs. The training-free approach and the use of tree prompts can inspire new research directions for leveraging LLMs in graph machine learning.

**Score: 8.5**

**Justification:**

The paper makes a substantial contribution by proposing a novel and effective approach to GSL for TAGs in the era of LLMs. The method is well-motivated, innovative, and supported by comprehensive experiments. The decoupled, training-free architecture addresses a crucial challenge in the field by making GSL more efficient and adaptable. While the dependency on LLMs and the number of hyperparameters are potential limitations, the overall novelty and significance of the approach merit a high score. The clear performance gains, scalability, and flexibility position this paper as a valuable contribution that can influence future research in GSL and graph machine learning.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### LLaVA-CMoE: Towards Continual Mixture of Experts for Large Vision-Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21227v1)
- **Authors**: Hengyuan Zhao, Ziqin Wang, Qixin Sun, Kaiyou Song, Yilin Li, Xiaolin Hu, Qingpei Guo, Si Liu
- **Abstract**: Although applying Mixture of Experts to large language models for learning new tasks is widely regarded as an effective strategy for continuous learning, there still remain two major challenges: (1) As the number of tasks grows, simple parameter expansion strategies can lead to excessively large models. (2) Modifying the parameters of the existing router results in the erosion of previously acquired knowledge. In this paper, we present an innovative framework named LLaVA-CMoE, which is a continuous Mixture of Experts (MoE) architecture without any replay data. Specifically, we have developed a method called Probe-Guided Knowledge Extension (PGKE), which employs probe experts to assess whether additional knowledge is required for a specific layer. This approach enables the model to adaptively expand its network parameters based on task distribution, thereby significantly improving the efficiency of parameter expansion. Additionally, we introduce a hierarchical routing algorithm called Probabilistic Task Locator (PTL), where high-level routing captures inter-task information and low-level routing focuses on intra-task details, ensuring that new task experts do not interfere with existing ones. Our experiments shows that our efficient architecture has substantially improved model performance on the Coin benchmark while maintaining a reasonable parameter count.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the LLaVA-CMoE paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLaVA-CMoE, a novel continual learning framework for large vision-language models.  It aims to address the challenges of catastrophic forgetting and excessive model size when learning new tasks sequentially. The key contributions are:

1.  **Probe-Guided Knowledge Extension (PGKE):** This mechanism uses "probe experts" to determine which layers need to be expanded with new parameters for a given task.  It adaptively expands the model based on the specific task's requirements, leading to more efficient parameter allocation.

2.  **Probabilistic Task Locator (PTL):** This module is a hierarchical routing algorithm that captures inter-task relationships at a high level and intra-task details at a low level. It uses a VAE to extract task distribution representations, enabling the model to select the appropriate router and experts for a given input, even when the task category is unknown during inference.  This helps prevent new task experts from interfering with existing ones.

The authors evaluate LLaVA-CMoE on the CoIN benchmark, demonstrating improved performance in continual learning scenarios while maintaining a reasonable parameter count.

**Critical Evaluation:**
