- **Score**: 7/10

### Video-R1: Reinforcing Video Reasoning in MLLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21776v1)
- **Authors**: Kaituo Feng, Kaixiong Gong, Bohao Li, Zonghao Guo, Yibing Wang, Tianshuo Peng, Benyou Wang, Xiangyu Yue
- **Abstract**: Inspired by DeepSeek-R1's success in eliciting reasoning abilities through rule-based reinforcement learning (RL), we introduce Video-R1 as the first attempt to systematically explore the R1 paradigm for eliciting video reasoning within multimodal large language models (MLLMs). However, directly applying RL training with the GRPO algorithm to video reasoning presents two primary challenges: (i) a lack of temporal modeling for video reasoning, and (ii) the scarcity of high-quality video-reasoning data. To address these issues, we first propose the T-GRPO algorithm, which encourages models to utilize temporal information in videos for reasoning. Additionally, instead of relying solely on video data, we incorporate high-quality image-reasoning data into the training process. We have constructed two datasets: Video-R1-COT-165k for SFT cold start and Video-R1-260k for RL training, both comprising image and video data. Experimental results demonstrate that Video-R1 achieves significant improvements on video reasoning benchmarks such as VideoMMMU and VSI-Bench, as well as on general video benchmarks including MVBench and TempCompass, etc. Notably, Video-R1-7B attains a 35.8% accuracy on video spatial reasoning benchmark VSI-bench, surpassing the commercial proprietary model GPT-4o. All codes, models, data are released.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Video-R1: Reinforcing Video Reasoning in MLLMs" introduces a novel approach to enhance video reasoning capabilities in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). Inspired by DeepSeek-R1's success in text reasoning, the authors propose Video-R1, which utilizes rule-based reinforcement learning (RL) tailored for video data.  The core contributions include: 1) the T-GRPO algorithm, which explicitly encourages temporal reasoning by comparing performance on ordered versus shuffled video frames; 2) the creation of two new datasets, Video-R1-COT-165k and Video-R1-260k, comprising both image and video reasoning examples; and 3) empirical evidence demonstrating significant improvements on video reasoning benchmarks, even surpassing the performance of GPT-4o on the VSI-Bench dataset.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper exhibits a moderate degree of novelty.  The application of the R1 paradigm to the video domain is a clear contribution, as is the development of the T-GRPO algorithm. The design of the T-GRPO is technically sound and addresses a key deficiency in directly applying existing RL methods to video. While RL for MLLMs is an active area, the specific focus on temporal reasoning with a contrastive reward mechanism is a valuable addition. Creating new datasets to address data scarcity, especially high-quality reasoning datasets, is always a worthwhile endeavor.

*   **Significance:**  The paper's significance stems from its focus on a challenging and important problem: enabling MLLMs to perform sophisticated video reasoning.  The empirical results demonstrate that Video-R1 achieves state-of-the-art performance on some benchmarks, indicating a substantial advance in this area. The fact that a 7B model outperforms GPT-4o on a specific benchmark (VSI-Bench) is a strong selling point.  Furthermore, the analysis of the RL training dynamics (e.g., the emergence of "aha moments") provides interesting insights into how the model learns to reason. The ablation studies effectively isolate the contributions of the T-GRPO algorithm and the mixed image/video training data. The paper also identifies important limitations (e.g., limited video frames, computational constraints) and outlines promising directions for future research.

*   **Weaknesses:** The paper mentions that they train the model for only 1k RL steps due to computational resource limitations. This is a significant constraint. It is possible that with more training, the model's performance could improve even further. Although the authors mention that even with the limited training budget they observe significant improvements, more training would likely yield greater gains. More analysis of the model's failure cases would also be beneficial. Furthermore, it's crucial to interpret the VSI-Bench results carefully. While outperforming GPT-4o is impressive, it's essential to examine the types of questions where Video-R1 excels and where it still struggles. The generality of the improvement needs further scrutiny.
*  **Clarity:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow. The experimental setup and results are clearly presented.

* **Impact:** This work highlights the importance of using reinforcement learning paradigms to address video reasoning in MLLMs. The methods described in the paper can be used as a strong foundation to build new MLLMs for video processing.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a sound approach to improving video reasoning in MLLMs. The T-GRPO algorithm, the curated datasets, and the empirical results all support this claim. The work demonstrates a clear improvement over existing methods and identifies limitations that can guide future research. Although there are computational limitations and more training is desired, the paper's novelty and significant results make it a valuable contribution to the field. The paper's focus on temporal reasoning is also a well-justified area for exploration and it is likely that more papers will be written in this area in the future.

### VideoMage: Multi-Subject and Motion Customization of Text-to-Video Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21781v1)
- **Authors**: Chi-Pin Huang, Yen-Siang Wu, Hung-Kai Chung, Kai-Po Chang, Fu-En Yang, Yu-Chiang Frank Wang
- **Abstract**: Customized text-to-video generation aims to produce high-quality videos that incorporate user-specified subject identities or motion patterns. However, existing methods mainly focus on personalizing a single concept, either subject identity or motion pattern, limiting their effectiveness for multiple subjects with the desired motion patterns. To tackle this challenge, we propose a unified framework VideoMage for video customization over both multiple subjects and their interactive motions. VideoMage employs subject and motion LoRAs to capture personalized content from user-provided images and videos, along with an appearance-agnostic motion learning approach to disentangle motion patterns from visual appearance. Furthermore, we develop a spatial-temporal composition scheme to guide interactions among subjects within the desired motion patterns. Extensive experiments demonstrate that VideoMage outperforms existing methods, generating coherent, user-controlled videos with consistent subject identities and interactions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces VideoMage, a novel framework for customizing text-to-video diffusion models to incorporate multiple user-specified subject identities and their interactive motions. VideoMage utilizes subject and motion LoRAs to capture personalized content from user-provided images and videos.  A key innovation is an appearance-agnostic motion learning approach that disentangles motion patterns from visual appearance, using negative classifier-free guidance. A spatial-temporal composition scheme guides interactions among the subjects within the desired motion patterns, using gradient-based fusion and spatial attention regularization. Experiments demonstrate that VideoMage outperforms existing methods in generating coherent, user-controlled videos with consistent subject identities and interactions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's key novelty lies in its unified approach to handling *both* multiple subject identities and their interactive motions within text-to-video generation. Previous methods often focused on personalizing only a single concept (either subject or motion) and struggled with appearance leakage when trying to combine them. The appearance-agnostic motion learning using negative classifier-free guidance is also a notable contribution. The spatial-temporal composition scheme builds on existing attention mechanisms, but its application to multi-subject interaction is novel.

*   **Significance:**  The significance stems from the increased control and flexibility it offers users in video content creation. By allowing users to define both *who* is in the video and *how* they interact, VideoMage opens up possibilities for more creative and personalized video generation compared to methods constrained to static subject identities or isolated motion patterns. If the framework performs robustly across a range of subjects and motion types, it could have a real impact.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Unified framework:** Handles both subject identity and motion customization.
    *   **Appearance-agnostic motion learning:** Effectively disentangles motion from visual appearance.
    *   **Spatial-temporal composition:** Provides a mechanism for controlling subject interactions.
    *   **Strong experimental results:** Outperforms existing methods on key metrics and in human evaluations.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Cost:** The paper acknowledges the limitations in customizing long motion sequences and creating extended videos due to computational demands, which is a limitation that is common in current methods and may limit practical deployment.
    *   **Qualitative Evaluation:** While the qualitative results in the paper are compelling, there is a need for a more standardized and controlled way to evaluate the quality of the generated videos, especially concerning the consistency of subject appearance and motion over longer sequences.
    *   **Potential for Bias Amplification:** The dependency on LoRAs may lead to a magnification of bias in the training data used for the original diffusion model as well as the datasets used to train the LoRAs.

*   **Potential Influence:** If validated and further refined, VideoMage could influence research directions in text-to-video generation by setting a new benchmark for multi-subject and motion customization. It could also be incorporated into video editing tools, providing users with greater creative control.

**Justification of Score:**

While the paper addresses a significant challenge in the text-to-video generation field and introduces novel techniques, it also has some limitations and potential biases to consider. The appearance-agnostic learning and spatial-temporal composition scheme represent tangible improvements in the area, making the work more than just an incremental step.
